- **FE-1: a registry scan hands each file's text to the engine once** (Oct 7; John put it first: "Let's prioritize the how files reach the engine and project scans stories and move those up to top of our list"; plan `specs/lazaret-files-to-engine-plan-2026-10-07.md`, P-13, P-6's first half, P-8). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/texts.rs` (new: the store, the process's, behind a lock, bounded at 1 GiB; `decode_wtf8` moved here from lazaret-ffi; `put` cuts the region by code points and checks it as it cuts, all or none; `release`, `held`), `api.rs` (`call` reads `text_id` from the store, on the calling thread; `texts.put` (the batch form), `texts.drop`, `texts.info`; `cross_file`'s `text_ids`; `texts.*` refused in a batch on threads), `lazaret-ffi` (`texts.put` kept as the request's bytes, before they are read into code points), `scanner/engine.py` (`Texts`: each distinct text put once, by content, and named by id; `close`; a refused put makes the scan send its texts with its calls, as before; `call_answers`, `import_time_risks`, `cross_file_answer` take it; `spawned_scripts_many` and `agent_hijacks`, a batch at a time), `registry/contentcache.py` (`Digests`; `file_key` and `cross_file_key` take one), `registry/repo.py` (`_ArtifactScan.texts`, `.digests`, `release()` at the end of `finish` and in `scan_members`' `finally`; the import-time code asks what each file starts a batch at a time (`_prefetch_spawned`); the agent check a batch at a time, the deadline checked before each; `_node_candidates` once per path), `scanner/core.py` (`dependency_agent_issue` takes the engine's answer). Tests `test_engine_texts.py` (new: the answers by id are the answers with the text, for every kind of text a str holds; a text crosses once; `close`; a store that refuses; what the engine refuses; the cross-file follower by id; a scan: each text once, nothing left in the store, also when the scan raises midway, the same answers without the store), `texts.rs` (3) and lazaret-ffi's (`texts_are_kept_and_named_by_id`); `test_content_memo.py`, `test_review_scan_deadline.py`, `test_use_risk.py` and `test_review_size_limit.py` updated for the new argument and for the agent check's batches. Measured on six large releases (`$SP/fe1b/`), each scanned as the guard scans it: every text now crosses once (most crossed three to five times); calls 264 to 69 (prettier), 1,859 to 447 (eslint), 1,797 to 96 (sympy), 11,972 to 1,811 (monaco-editor), 7,483 to 1,004 (next), 3,200 to 183 (litellm's wheel); request bytes 50.4 to 10.0 MB, 15.5 to 3.1, 93.4 to 26.5, 356 to 96, 247 to 71, 178 to 46. Quiet machine, fresh processes, median of three: 3.99 to 3.31 s, 1.29 to 1.21, 4.59 to 3.98, 19.6 to 17.4, 15.0 to 13.7, 10.8 to 9.6 (7 to 17% less); peak memory 254 to 278 MB, 110 to 106, 331 to 324, 1,144 to 1,030, 1,025 to 776, 790 to 676 (the texts no longer cross in JSON, read into four bytes a character in the engine; the store holds them as UTF-8). Not done: the npm package (it scans many files only in `--deps` and project scans: Q-1's turn), and step 4 (analyses shared between the steps), to be decided from a profile of the engine's time by call.
- **tiny_https's drop of Oct 7: the security review's fixes** (Oct 7; John: "Before running full gates let's integrate the new tiny https code"). `rust/crates/tiny_https` from `tiny_https-2026-10-07.tgz` (the library's folder on John's machine that day: 13 files changed, 4 added, none removed; SHA-256 `ef8dbd83…`): its B-93 to B-96 (two rounds of outside review, each claim checked against OpenSSL, Go's `crypto/x509` and Go's `note`; fixed: an exponential path search, now at most 256 signature checks; a version 3 anchor without `basicConstraints`; names that escaped name constraints, directory-name constraints, names in intermediates; a BOOLEAN `01`; a sumdb lookup answering another question; a leap second; the timing harness's repeat rule), `crypto::bignum` crate-private, and `tests/real_chains.rs` (chains captured from real servers, skipped until the captures are in `tests/data/real_chains/`). No interface `lazaret-net`, `lazaret-verify` or `lazaret-ffi` use changed. `scripts/sync_tiny_https.py` also takes `SECURITY_REVIEW.md` (the brief for the library's independent review, B-23); CI runs `real_chains` with the library's other offline tests. Gates: `check_rust_deps`, the copy verified, the build, tiny_https's tests (1,207), lazaret-net's, -verify's and -ffi's (39), the WebAssembly check, and the Python modules on the network and verification calls. Next from the library: TLS 1.2 (npm's edge refuses TLS 1.3 from John's machine, so npm's requests go through Python's transport there today; this sandbox's egress gateway terminates TLS, so its results say nothing about npm's edge).
- **tiny_https's second drop of Oct 7: TLS 1.2 for a server that speaks nothing newer** (Oct 7; John: "Tiny https agent reports npm requires tls 1.2 and we're amending the library to support that", then the drop: "Updated tiny https supporting TLS 1.2, Cookies, and GZIP"). `rust/crates/tiny_https` from `tiny_https-2026-10-07b.tgz` (423 files taken; SHA-256 `1dd602de…`): its B-36 (TLS 1.2 under the seven rules Lazaret asked for: RFC 8446's downgrade check, ECDHE with AEAD suites only, the extended master secret required, no RSA key exchange, CBC, renegotiation, compression, resumption or SHA-1 signatures, the certificate checks of 1.3, a minimum version per client and per request, the version in every response; the default minimum is 1.2), B-37 (an own `inflate` with limits on size and ratio, `Content-Encoding` decoding, a cookie jar, `Expect: 100-continue`, all opt-in), B-97 (its first field run on a normal network: 43 of 49 live hosts, the six others TLS 1.2-only, npm among them; 52 real chains, now fixtures), B-98 and B-99 (its timing harness on a coarse clock). `error::Error` is `#[non_exhaustive]` with a new `Decode`. `lazaret-net`: the new variant (and any later one) classified, `Reply::tls` and `Stream::tls` ("TLS 1.3" / "TLS 1.2"), the C ABI's answer carries `"tls"`, its docs; `Failure::TlsVersion` is now a server that speaks neither version (alert 70, or a version below the minimum). `nativenet`: `Reply.tls` and `Stream.tls`, the texts. Lazaret asks for none of the opt-in extras (no decompression: a body comes as sent; no cookies; no 100-continue) and leaves the minimum at the library's default, 1.2: a TLS 1.3 server still gets 1.3, and the downgrade check makes a 1.2 forced on a 1.3 server fail. `scripts/sync_tiny_https.py`'s note says so; CI runs the library's new `inflate_vectors` with its other offline tests. Tests: `test_nativenet.py`, a TLS 1.2-only server (Python's `ssl`, OpenSSL 3.0.13) answered natively over TLS 1.2 whole and streamed, a server that speaks neither version (a protocol_version alert) left to Python, on the first URL and on a redirect, and a host left to Python fetched by urllib; lazaret-net's `tls` on its replies and the new variant's kind. Gates: the copy verified, the build, tiny_https's unit tests (1,260; 17 ignored) and its offline integration tests (go_vectors, cms_vectors, sigstore_real, sigstore_synthetic, rekor_real, real_chains 6, inflate_vectors 2), its 47 OpenSSL interop tests here (all pass: the TLS 1.2 ones, the two men in the middle among them), lazaret-net's 26 and -ffi's 8. Still to confirm on John's network: npm over native TLS 1.2 (this sandbox's egress gateway terminates TLS and speaks 1.3).
- **Q-1: a project scan is the engine's, whole** (Oct 7; the second item John put first: "Let's prioritize the how files reach the engine and project scans stories and move those up to top of our list"; plan `specs/lazaret-project-scans-plan-2026-10-07.md`). The engine's half: `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/project.rs` (new: project mode after the rules, in core's order: the SQL statements without WHERE, the taint, the SQL built from strings into `execute()`, the function metrics, then the reviewers' markers and the cap), `taint.rs` (new: the intra-file taint pass; a taint configuration read from the call's arguments and checked: a pattern that does not compile, a category no one knows, a sink that is not `[pattern, category]` are refused), `scanfile.rs` (`scan_project`), `api.rs` (`scan_file` with `dep: false` is the whole of a project file's scan; `taint_scan`, `functions`), `filectx.rs` (`literals()`), the pack (19 values: the taint model's tables); tests `project_tests.rs` (10), and three recorded sets recorded from core's answers before anything moved (`test_snapshot_project.py`, `project_corpus.py`: `scan_project`, 3,005 outputs; `scan_project_configured`; `functions`). The switch: `scanner/core.py` (`_project_passes`, the taint pass and its helpers, the SQL passes, `extract_functions`' body, the markers and the cap retired; `taint_args` and `apply_taint_config` keep a configuration per language for the calls; `_FileCtx` is the config scan's alone), `scanner/engine.py` (`scan_calls`: `scan_file` in both modes, with the taint configuration in project mode; a call left unanswered is SC-TRUNCATED alone), `registry/repo.py`, `scanner/frameworks.py` retired (`pyflow/frameworks.rs` decides for both taint passes); the npm package's `scan.js` (`scanFile` asks the engine in both modes; `taintScan` wraps `taint_scan`), `native.js` (`scanProjectFile`, `taintFindings`), `taint.js`, `sql.js` and `functions.js` retired. 3,501 lines of source out, 157 in. The passes' 30 seconds of wall clock a file are the engine's work budget now, so a file gets the same findings on every machine. Tests: `test_wasm_parity_project.py` (new: the WebAssembly build against the native library on the three sets and on `taint_scan`), `taint_tests.rs` (7: the helpers core's tests held), `pyflow/frameworks.rs`'s own (2), and the Python and npm tests that called the retired code moved to the engine's calls (`test_review_clip_backstop.py`: the work budget, not the clock; `test_engine_scan_file.py`: the configuration goes with the call; `test_pyflow_frameworks.py`: both passes' route parameters). Parity, finding by finding: the project corpus with and without a configuration, the scanner tests' inputs, the scan_file corpus, Python's standard library, this repository, 5,295 Go and Rust files and eleven adversarial shapes; `lazaret scan`'s and the npm CLI's reports the same before and after, and the same as each other, on `python/src`, `js/src` and twelve stdlib packages. Measured (`core.scan_project`, a quiet machine, best of three, before and after interleaved): `python/src` (69 files) 2.69 to 1.07 s, `js/src` (31) 0.53 to 0.32, twelve stdlib packages (171) 2.79 to 1.01; the passes took 1.70 to 1.94 s in Python (63 to 68% of a scan) and take about 0.2 s in the engine. The engine's calls (the rules and the passes, 0.35 to 0.41 s, and the cross-file flow, which was the engine's already, 0.19 to 0.21 s) are now about 55% of a scan. The largest piece left in Python was the metrics (`compute_metrics`, each file lexed again: 0.14 to 0.19 s, 13 to 18% of a scan): the plan's step 4, built since (below).
- **Q-1, step 4: a project's line metrics are the engine's** (Oct 7, night; the plan's step 4, which Q-1's measurement named the largest piece left in Python). `core.compute_metrics` lexed each file again in a second engine call (`lex_comment_spans`, the text crossing again), mapped the comments onto its lines and keyed each window of six lines of code by joining them, in Python; the npm package did the same with its own lexer (`lexLines`). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/metrics.rs` (new): a file's comment lines, lines of code and measured lines, and each window's key, a 64-bit FNV-1a hash of its six stripped lines joined, as core keyed a window; core's lines (the text pinned, split at "\n" alone: a JavaScript U+2028 does not end a line there, as it does for the rules). From a project scan's own reading (`scan_file` with `"metrics": true` answers `{"issues", "metrics"}`; `metrics::of_ctx` tallies the scan's own lines and comment layout, and reads the file again only where they are not core's: a CR in the text, a line separator in JavaScript), or on their own (`file_metrics`). `scanner/engine.py`: `scan_files_metrics` (the scan and the metrics of a batch in one call each), `file_metrics`; `scanner/core.py`: `scan_project` keeps each file's `lineMetrics`, and `compute_metrics` compares the windows' keys across the files (the rest is the engine's); a file the engine gives no answer for counts its non-blank lines as code (review B3), its windows not compared. The npm package's `computeMetrics` (`js/src/scanner/metrics.js`) asks `file_metrics` for each file (`native.js` `fileMetrics`) and merges the same way; its `lexLines` stays for config files. Held to core's code as it was (`test_project_metrics.py`, new, which keeps it as `REFERENCE`): 360 sets of files built from every kind of comment, string, blank line, line ending, unassigned code point and repeat in each language, this repository's scanner, npm package and fixtures, and a project scan of 20 made-up sets (no file read again); the npm package's merge the same as core's on 150 sets; while built, 1,800 sets, 150 scanned trees and five real trees, none different; `test_wasm_parity_project.py` (the WebAssembly build on 802 files' metrics and 201 scans with them), `metrics.rs`'s tests (6; the scan's reading and the file's own give the same metrics), `test_review_cli.py` (a file with no answer counts as code, now on the engine's answer). Measured (`core.scan_project`, best of three, in one process, before and after alternated twice): this repository's `python/src` (69 files) 0.769 to 0.674 s (-12%), `js/src` 0.192 to 0.173 (-9%), asyncio 0.128 to 0.109 (-15%), email 0.094 to 0.080 (-15%), urllib3 0.114 to 0.088 (-23%), requests 0.050 to 0.045 (-10%); `compute_metrics` on a project scan's files is the windows' comparison alone (about 5 ms on `python/src`), and on files without their scan's metrics 0.094 to 0.067 s. Fresh processes' scans of the small trees did not move beyond their noise (startup is 0.2 s of them).
- **A project scan's redaction sweep takes what the engine did not redact** (Oct 8, early; seen measuring step 4: `redact_file_issues` took 0.137 s of a 0.670 s scan of `python/src`). The sweep gives findings built from a file's raw lines the file's own redaction (its entropy literals, its PEM blocks), and `scan_project` handed it every finding of the engine's scans too, so it read every file with a finding again in Python (its literal set built line by line) to redact what the engine had redacted already, as `mk_issue` does, when it built them. It takes the flows' and the dependency checks' findings alone now, as the npm package's `redactFlowIssues` always has. Whole results, every field of every finding, the same on 14 trees (4,112 findings, 153 of them secrets: this repository's tests, npm tests, scripts, fixtures and sources, five stdlib packages, urllib3 and requests). `python/src`: the sweep 0.137 to 0.034 s, the scan 0.670 to 0.571 s (-15%; best of five in one process). Test: `test_review_redaction_by_file.py` (`test_only_what_the_engine_did_not_redact_is_swept`).
- **A config or data file's lines are read where a test can report** (Oct 8, early; seen measuring the sweep: `scan_config_file` took 0.10 s of a `python/src` scan, nearly all of it on `popular_names.json`, 547 KB in 22,427 lines). The scan ran the token pattern and S-SECRET's key-value test on every line, in Python. Now one pass over the whole text finds the lines the token pattern matches, and one over the text lowered finds those where a credential-named key meets a separator (`configsecrets.SECRET_KV_HINT_RE`: the name ends as `SECRET_KEY_RE`'s names end; a key is ASCII, so lowering it is exact, and an alternation `re.I` searches is six times slower); a line in neither, with no URL, is passed over (a .netrc's are all read). No pattern there matches across a newline or looks at one but as it looks at a line's end, so a line holds the same matches either way. `_jwt_search` returns at once when no `.eyJ` follows (every match holds one). The three config files of `python/src`: 0.103 to 0.034 s. The same findings as before on 4,726 config and data files (726 from this machine's packages, tests and /etc, 4,000 made up from every form the scan reads: 21,260 findings). Tests: `test_config_secrets.py` (`LinesReadTests`: every credential-named key a key-value pair can hold has the hint, in 10,000 keys and forms; 300 made-up files give what reading every line gives).
- **TLS 1.2 is the floor on both transports** (Oct 7; John: "Realistically we should avoid any tls < 1.2 as that would be horrible security stance by a provider"). Nothing connected below TLS 1.2 before either: tiny_https speaks 1.3 and 1.2, and the server that speaks neither went to Python's transport, whose default refuses it on every Python Lazaret supports (3.10 on), but only because Python says so. `scanner/nativenet.py`: such a server is refused (`NetError` "tls", naming the hop's host: "… speaks neither TLS 1.3 nor TLS 1.2, and Lazaret uses nothing older"), not handed to Python, and the per-process list of hosts left to Python (`python_hosts`) is gone; `TLS_FLOOR`, `tls_context()` (urllib's default context, PEP 476's hook, with the floor stated and HTTP/1.1 offered as urllib offers it) and `HTTPSHandler` (urllib's, on that context, made at the first request). Every urllib opener takes the handler (`registry/repo.py`'s two, `guard.py`'s, `sources.py`'s, `scanner/sca_feeds.py`'s), `registry/keepalive.py` the context, and `scanner/secretverify_http.py`, which imports nothing from Lazaret, its own `default_context()` with the same floor (`pg/` already refused anything below 1.2). `lazaret-net`'s docs. Tests `test_tls_floor.py` (new: a local server that speaks TLS 1.0 and 1.1 only, at OpenSSL's security level 0; with Python's defaults lowered as a process might lower them, urllib's own opener speaks TLS 1.1 to it and Lazaret's openers, kept-alive connections and secret transport refuse it; the floor raises a lowered default and keeps a higher one; a source check holds every opener, HTTPS connection and TLS context under `python/src` to the floor), `test_nativenet.py` (the server below the floor refused, the second time too, and by the registry's fetch without urllib; on a redirect, by the hop's name), `test_guard_native_fetch.py` (its test root given through urllib's default hook, which the floor builds on). Python 3.10, 3.11 and 3.13.
- **gzip for registry documents** (Oct 7; John: "Supports gunzip", then "Add after Q-1"). `lazaret-net`: `Request::decompress` (a document: tiny_https's `decompress` with `max_decoded_bytes` at the request's budget and `max_decode_ratio(MAX_DECODE_RATIO = 200, DECODE_RATIO_FLOOR = 1 MiB)`, this request only), `Reply::decoded` and `Stream::decoded` (tiny_https's `uncompressed`), a decoded body over the budget is `TooLarge` (`Decode(OutputLimit)`), any other fault of a compressed body is `Http`; any other request asks for `identity`. `lazaret-ffi`: the description's `decompress`, the answer's `decoded`. `nativenet`: `GZIP_BUDGET` (64 MiB: every document's budget, the guard's too; a download's is 200 MiB and more), `gzip(max_bytes)`, `compressed=` on `request` and `open_stream`, `Reply.decoded`, `Stream.decoded`, `LAZARET_GZIP=0`; h2's threshold (`DOCUMENT_BUDGET`, 32 MiB) unchanged. Tests: lazaret-net's (5: a document asks for gzip and gets it decoded, read whole and streamed, and over HTTP/2; any other request gets the bytes as they came, `Content-Encoding` and all; a bomb past a budget under the ratio's floor is too large, under 64 MiB past the ratio, streamed too; exactly the budget decoded is fine and the wire's bytes are held to it too; a stream cut short or followed by bytes is the server's error), lazaret-ffi's (the description's field), `test_nativenet.py` (a real packument's shape, 4.2 MB decoded from 0.96, through `request`, `open_stream` and the registry's fetch; a `.tgz` served with `Content-Encoding: gzip` comes byte for byte to a download; the budget, the ratio and a cut stream, whole and streamed; which budgets ask, and `LAZARET_GZIP`). Measured (`$SP/gz/`): fourteen large documents (eight npm packuments, four PyPI, two crates.io index files) are 124.3 MB whole and 15.9 MB compressed (npm 5.1 to 13.2 times smaller, PyPI 4.5 to 5.7, crates.io's index served uncompressed either way). From this data center, with a direct link to npm's edge (39 MB in 0.27 s), the set took longer compressed: 2.9 to 4.6 s against 1.1 to 1.6 s one at a time, 1.3 to 1.8 against 0.35 to 0.7 s from 8 threads (three rounds, both protocols): Cloudflare compresses a full packument as it sends it, about 35 MB/s (vite: 1.26 s compressed, 0.27 s whole, both cache hits); PyPI's documents took the same time either way, and the client's decoding is not the cost (served locally, vite's 39 MB took 0.19 s compressed and 0.08 s whole). Through a local relay capping the link at 100 Mbit/s: 3.6 against 17.4 s one at a time, 1.5 against 10.5 s from 8 threads; at 300 Mbit/s, 3.2 against 9.8 s and 1.25 against 4.2 s. Compressed, HTTP/1.1 was as fast as h2 or faster in every round, so the guard's documents (64 MiB) stay on it.
- **V-1, stage 2, step 1: live secret verification's table and logic are the engine's** (Oct 7; John's decision 7: the provider table and the logic in the engine, one copy for both packages, each package making only the HTTPS call; decision 4: "Let's follow trufflehog behavior. Verify is an explicit flag -- detection of secret is default without verification it is live"; plan `specs/lazaret-v1-stage2-plan-2026-10-07.md`). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/secrets.rs` (new: the pack's `_VERIFY_PROVIDERS` read and checked once by the rules of stage 1's `validate` (`provider_of`, `rules_of`); `identify`; `request` (the parts checked: the provider's own, each printable ASCII, at most 512 characters, all of it the part's pattern; the fields that carry the credential named in `secret_headers`; AWS's Signature Version 4 over `lazaret-verify`'s SHA-256, with HMAC (RFC 2104) here, since tiny_https keeps its own behind the network feature); `judge` (the first rule that holds; a body cut short read by its status alone; JSON only when whole and UTF-8, a key's last value counting; the owner's name printable, at most 80 characters, a part of the credential `[redacted]`)), `api.rs` (`secrets.providers`, `secrets.identify`, `secrets.request`, `secrets.judge`), `json.rs` (a number as RFC 8259 has it: `01`, `1.`, `.5` and `-` refused, as Python's json refuses them), the pack (`_VERIFY_PROVIDERS`: stage 1's seven providers as they were), `Cargo.toml` (`lazaret-verify`, tiny_https's pure part: the engine's one dependency). Python: `scanner/secretverify.py` on the engine's calls (`identify`, `build_request`, `interpret`; the table read from the pack when first asked for; `validate` stays, and `scripts/make_rust_tables.py --check` holds the pack's table to it); `secretverify_providers.py` and `sigv4.py` retired (215 lines), with `test_sigv4.py` (its vectors are the engine's tests now). Tests: `secrets_tests.rs` (17: AWS's published SigV4 vectors and stage 1's signature, RFC 4231's HMAC cases 1, 2 and 6, an entry checked as `validate` checks it, the formats and the 512-character limit, each provider's request, the answers, hostile bodies, a part of the credential looked for in all of the owner's name), `json.rs`'s (`numbers_are_rfc_8259s`), `test_secretverify.py` (89, stage 1's, through the engine; a table whose headers are not a mapping is refused, where stage 1's `validate` raised AttributeError), `test_secretverify_http.py` (55, unchanged), `test_wasm_parity_secrets.py` (new: the WebAssembly build against the native library on 1,706 calls: the table, `identify`, every provider's requests and refusals, AWS's signature at three times, every provider's rules on 19 bodies, 7 statuses, whole and cut). Held to stage 1's Python, kept aside, on 53,760 answers, 2,800 requests (AWS's signatures at random times among them) and 5,500 texts: no difference. Two differences on purpose: a NaN or an Infinity in an answer is not JSON (stage 1's Python read one), and a part of the credential is looked for in all of the owner's name an answer gives before it is cut to 80 characters (stage 1 looked in the first 320, so a part over 240 characters long that began in the first 80 kept its start; parts that overlap are one `[redacted]`). The WebAssembly build is 74 KB larger (5,286,014 bytes to 5,360,243; gzipped, 1,843,317 to 1,869,608). Nothing calls it yet: step 2 is Python's transport over lazaret-net.
- **V-1, stage 2, step 2: a secret being verified goes over lazaret-net** (Oct 7; John's decision 7: Python's call over lazaret-net, npm's over Node; plan `specs/lazaret-v1-stage2-plan-2026-10-07.md`). `scanner/secretverify_http.py`: `native_transport` (`nativenet.open_stream` with the provider's host as the only host, no redirect, nothing compressed, the call's timeout as the limit on the whole request and checked again between reads; the body read to `max_bytes` and cut, the request opened with a budget of 1 GiB (`NATIVE_BUDGET`) so that an answer that declares more than `max_bytes` is cut, not refused before its status is known; the fields the engine names (`Request.secret_headers`) go as `Credential`s for that host and the request alone (`first`), the others as plain headers; lazaret-net's failures as the transport's kinds, never in its words (`_native_error`)); `default_transport` (lazaret-net's, and urllib's for a request the native client is not to send: `UsePython` for no library, `LAZARET_NETWORK=python`, a proxy reached over TLS, proxy settings the two would read differently); a 301, 302, 303, 307 or 308 that names a Location is `TransportError("redirect")` on both (urllib's returned it as an answer, read as unknown; tiny_https cannot hand over one it does not follow); `Request.secret_headers` (checked: fields of the request). `secretverify.py`: `build_request` names the fields, the default transport, "redirect" in a result's words. `lazaret-net`: `PLAIN_HEADERS` gains `anthropic-version` and `x-amz-date`, the fields verification sends beside a credential (`x-amz-date` says nothing without the signature, which goes as a credential). Tests: `test_secretverify_http.py` (92, from 55: the request, redirect, size and time tests on both transports, lazaret-net's reaching the stub through its CONNECT proxy with the stub's root as its trust anchor; each provider's request arrives as the engine made it, each field once and the credential in its own fields alone; AWS's signature holds over the request as the stub received it, worked out in the test with hashlib and hmac, on both; an answer that declares 3 MiB is cut at the budget with its status; a credential field not named as one is refused, not sent; an untrusted root and another host's certificate are `tls`; the proxy's tunnel, its credentials and its refusals; the default transport's choice; lazaret-net's failures as the transport's kinds and none of its words), `test_secretverify.py` (98: the stub tests over lazaret-net too, and a `Verifier`'s default transport is lazaret-net's; each provider's fields: the credential's named, the others in lazaret-net's `PLAIN_HEADERS`, read from its source), `_verify_stub.py` (a root and a certificate under it, which tiny_https takes as urllib does; `native`, `OverLazaretNet`; each field as it came, `Seen.raw`), lazaret-net's `a_header_a_request_does_not_set_is_refused` (the two new names). README and CHANGELOG (NET-1's entry: a secret being verified goes over lazaret-net, to its provider's host alone), `docs/DESIGN.md`, STRUCTURE.md.
- **V-1, stage 2, step 3: `lazaret scan --verify-secrets`** (Oct 7; John's decision 4: "Let's follow trufflehog behavior. Verify is an explicit flag -- detection of secret is default without verification it is live"; plan `specs/lazaret-v1-stage2-plan-2026-10-07.md`). The engine: `secrets.find` (`secrets.rs`: the credentials a file's flagged lines hold, each once with its lines: a word, a run of `[A-Za-z0-9_+/-]` or a piece of one between slashes, that is all of a one-part provider's format; an AWS key id and secret key of the same file paired nearest lines first, at most `MAX_PAIRS` (8) pairs of `MAX_HALVES` (64) of each). `scanner/verifyscan.py` (new): `lines_of` (a secret finding's line read again from its file as the scan numbered it: decoded as the scan decodes it, U+2028 ending a JavaScript line, a regular file under the root and no larger than the scan reads, nothing else), `notice` (stderr, before the first call: the providers, how many credentials each and their hosts), `verify_findings` (one `secrets.find` a file, `Verifier.verify_all`, each finding the result of its line's credentials that says most: live, then unknown, then rejected; live makes it a BLOCKER vulnerability, an S-ENTROPY hotspot too, and names the account; rejected keeps it and says to revoke it anyway, the history keeps it; unknown keeps it and says why; `verification` in the result: the providers asked, the credentials' outcomes, the findings' and the secret findings no provider can be asked about). `core.py`: `--verify-secrets` (after the scan and the source block, before the baseline), `regrade` (the order, the counts, the security and reliability ratings, the two severity conditions and the gate, from the issues again; `build_result` shares `_severity_grades` with it), `verification_line` in the terminal and HTML reports, `verified` in a SARIF result's properties. `sourcescan.FLAG_OPTIONS`: the flag goes to the scan of a `github:` or `gitlab:` checkout. Tests: `secrets_tests.rs` (4 more: a credential is a word of its line, in quotes, after `=` or `:`, in a header, a URL's userinfo or the end of its path, and not a word that runs on past its format; AWS's pairs, nearest first, of one file's lines; the pairs bounded; a long line read in linear time), `test_verifyscan.py` (new, 16: the lines as the scan numbers them, and nothing the scan would not read; each finding's outcome, message and severity; AWS's pair asked about as one, never across files; a live S-ENTROPY hotspot graded again, and a result nothing changed in graded the same; a finding takes the most telling of its line's credentials; the note before the first call; nothing asked without a credential; nothing written; the CLI without the flag asks nothing; with it, the note, the report's line, the JSON, SARIF and HTML, and none of the values in stdout, stderr or any report; `--ci` fails on a live credential; the same scan over TLS against the stub provider on both transports, each credential reaching its own provider's host alone and AWS's secret key never sent), `test_wasm_parity_secrets.py` (`secrets.find` on 8 sets of lines), `test_sourcescan.py` (the flag goes to the checkout's scan). README (the flag and a paragraph), `python/README.md`, CHANGELOG (V-1's entry), `docs/DESIGN.md` (§5k, and §5j's urllib list), `docs/RUST_ENGINE.md`, STRUCTURE.md. Seen while building it: S-TOKEN does not know npm's, OpenAI's or Anthropic's formats, so such a token in code is an S-ENTROPY hotspot (or S-SECRET by its variable's name), which the flag raises to a BLOCKER when its provider says it is live; teaching S-TOKEN the table's formats is a detection item for the backlog.
- **V-1, stage 2, step 4: `npx lazaret --verify-secrets`, the npm package's runner** (Oct 7; John's decision 7: "npm over Node"; the TLS floor: "Realistically we should avoid any tls < 1.2 as that would be horrible security stance by a provider"). `js/src/verify.js` (new; the twin of `verifyscan.py`, `secretverify.py` and `secretverify_http.py`, on the same engine calls): `httpsTransport` (node:https, the provider's host alone on port 443, `minVersion: 'TLSv1.2'`, no redirect followed (a 3xx that names a Location is `redirect`), the body read to 64 KiB and cut with its status, one deadline, `Accept-Encoding: identity`; `HTTPS_PROXY` through an http:// proxy's CONNECT tunnel, the proxy's own credentials to the proxy alone, an https:// proxy refused and nothing sent, `NO_PROXY` read as urllib reads it; a failure as the transport's kinds, never Node's words), `checkRequest`, `Verifier` (the run's cache keyed as Python's, 120 s and 500 calls, two calls at once to a provider, a failed call unknown), `verifyFindings` (a finding's line from the text the scan read, `secrets.find` a file, the note before the first call, the same marks, `verification`, `regrade`). `report.js`: `regrade` (with `buildResult` on a shared `severityGrades`), `verificationLine` in the terminal report, `verified` in SARIF. `cli.js`: `--verify-secrets` (help and option); `run()` returns a promise of the exit code when it is given, and `bin/lazaret.js` waits for it. Found by its tests and fixed before delivery: a request with `agent: false` and a tunnel's socket connects on its own (Node gives an agent the connection, ignoring `createConnection`), so the first version went to the host directly, around the proxy, and Node writes `Host: host:443` for a request without an agent (its default port is then 80), which AWS's signature would not hold over: the tunnel's request has no agent and `defaultPort: 443`, and a test checks the Host field and that every request went through the proxy. Tests: `js/test/verify-secrets.test.js` (new, 16: a request refused before anything opens; the proxy settings; a request through the tunnel, the proxy seeing only the host, the credential once, the Host field the host alone; a POST's body and a request's own User-Agent; redirects; the size limit with the status; a slow, dripping, cut or closed answer; an untrusted root and another host's certificate; a server that speaks only TLS 1.1 refused, which a client without the floor reaches; the proxy's credentials, refusals and an https:// proxy; every provider's credential at its own host alone and AWS's signature holding over what the stub received; the cache and the format; the limits; a failed call's words; the CLI with and without the flag, the reports, and no value in any output; the help and the bin), `test_js_parity_verify.py` (new: both CLIs as processes against the same stub, the Python package over lazaret-net with `SSL_CERT_FILE`, node with `NODE_EXTRA_CA_CERTS`, both through `HTTPS_PROXY`: the same calls, the same findings, `verification`, counts, ratings, gate and exit code, and no value in either's output; run by `ci.yml`'s npm job). README, `js/README.md`, CHANGELOG (V-1's entry names both packages), `docs/DESIGN.md` (§5k, and the no-network goal's one exception), STRUCTURE.md.
- **V-1, stage 2: the table asks about API keys alone, and a key is rejected only on its provider's own word** (Oct 7; from John's two screenshots of OpenAI's and Anthropic's key formats, and Anthropic's pages on its keys: "Create an Admin API key" and "Set up the Compliance API"). Before: OpenAI's pattern took its admin keys (`sk-admin-`) and Anthropic's took every `sk-ant-` word, its OAuth tokens (`sk-ant-oat01-`) and admin keys (the Console's `sk-ant-admin01-`, Claude Enterprise's `sk-ant-api01-`, which are its Compliance Access Keys too) among them, and any 401 from either was "rejected": a live admin key that `/v1/models` turns away, or a 401 of a gateway's, would have been reported rejected. The pack's `_VERIFY_PROVIDERS`: OpenAI's `sk-(?!ant-|admin-)(?:proj-|svcacct-)?…` (user, project and service account keys), rejected only on a 401 with OpenAI's `error.code` `invalid_api_key`; Anthropic's `sk-ant-api03-…` (its API keys, as it documents them), rejected only on a 401 with its `error.type` `authentication_error`, and a 403 `permission_error` (a key it knows) live, as Stripe's is; any other 401 is unknown. What the table no longer asks about is detected as before, and not verified. Tests: `secrets_tests.rs` (`a_key_is_rejected_only_on_its_providers_own_word`; the formats: the OAuth tokens, admin keys and `sk-ant-api01-`/`02-` are no one's), `test_secretverify.py` (the answers: a 401 without the provider's word, or with another, is unknown; the formats), the stub plans and the scan tests' scripts answer with the providers' own bodies; `scripts/verifylive` (the made-up Anthropic key is an `sk-ant-api03-` one; its README says what each provider's made-up key must get back and which keys are not asked about); the fuzz target's dictionary (the new prefixes). Admin keys verified against their own APIs (OpenAI's and Anthropic's admin endpoints) are a backlog item (V-3), not this release's.
- **V-2: S-TOKEN knows npm's access tokens and OpenAI's and Anthropic's keys** (Oct 7; found building V-1's stage 2: such a token in code was an S-ENTROPY hotspot, or S-SECRET by its variable's name, and nothing when neither applied). `scanner/core.py`: three alternatives, each a whole run of its characters, before the JWT one in `_TOKEN_ALTS` and `_TOKEN_REDACT_ALTS`: `npm_` and 36 letters and digits; `sk-ant-`, a kind of three to five letters and two digits (an API key's `api03`, an admin key's `admin01`, an OAuth token's `oat01`) and 40 to 200 of `[A-Za-z0-9_-]`; `sk-`, 20 to 90 of them (a project's, a service account's or an admin's key names its kind there), `T3BlbkFJ` (the base64 of "OpenAI", in the middle of OpenAI's keys) and 20 to 74 more. Each starts with its literal and looks behind it (`npm_(?<![A-Za-z0-9]npm_)…`), as crates.io's now does too: the same language as a leading `(?<!…)`, which Python's `re` tries at every position (the alternatives' regex ran about five times slower with one, and core's token pattern takes about half the time it took, the three formats added; the engine's linre runs both forms alike). The pack (S-TOKEN's rule, `_TOKEN_PATTERN`, `_TOKEN_REDACT_PATTERN`, `_SECRET_LINE_PATTERNS`, `_TOKEN_RULE`; rule set 2.48.0), `registry/repo.py` (`ENGINE_VERSION`), the npm package's `findSecretToken` (`js/src/lib/redact.js`: an `npm_` head and an `sk-` head, Anthropic's form tried before OpenAI's, as the alternation does) and the dashboard's copy of it, its rule table, its redaction list and its CSP hash. Measured first: none of the formats is in the benchmark's 945 releases, the popular set's 1,205, Part F's 4,620 or the installed Python and npm packages here (every member of every archive searched, `$SP/v2/grep_formats.py`). Tests: `test_config_secrets.py` (`ProviderKeyFormats`: each format in Python, JavaScript, Go and Rust code and in config files, the whole run, what is not one (glued, too short or long, a kind of the wrong size, documentation's `sk-ant-api03-...`), redacted on another finding's line), `test_review_perf_regex.py` (the formats' pieces in the matcher's equivalence test), `test_js_parity_tokens.py` (new: the npm package's `findSecretToken` against core's regex, match by match in both modes, on 7,947 texts of the formats' pieces, each length at and around each limit and each neighbour; ten mutations of the matcher's limits and look-behinds each fail it; run by `ci.yml`'s npm job), `test_review_dashboard_perf_regex.py` (the page's twin on the same texts), `js/test/config-secrets.test.js`. The test tokens are built in pieces, so no source file holds a token-shaped literal: GitHub's push protection knows OpenAI's and Anthropic's formats.
- **BR-5: what a package's Python code imports is followed to the end** (Oct 8; the backlog's BR-5, reproduced first: a wheel whose `x/__init__.py` imports 299 modules, one of which imports `x/evil.py`, the whole environment sent away, lost evil.py's CRITICAL SC-IMPORT-RISK; with 5 it had it). `registry/repo.py`: `_python_reach` (the import-time walk: a wheel's or an sdist's top-level modules and what they import) stopped at 300 modules seen, and `_python_install_scripts` (what setup.py and an in-tree backend import: install scripts) at 200; both are `_py_walk` now, with no cap (what it holds is the archive's members; `_deadline` before each module is read, and every 4,096 names within one: past it the release is INCOMPLETE, `time`), each walk's `module()` resolving a name against its own roots (the install walk's now also the `backend-path` directories pip puts on `sys.path`). What a module names is `_py_import_targets`: every module of `import a, b` (the first alone was read), a statement after a `;` or a compound statement's `:` (`_PY_IMPORT_STMT_RE`, read on the lines that hold the word `import`: tried at every `;` and `:`, the regex took 1.9 s of a 92 MB release's walk, so read 0.1 s), a line a backslash continues, `from .import x` and `from x import(y)`, a parenthesized name list read line by line to its `)` with each line's comment cut (`_paren_list`: it was cut at 50 names or 2,000 characters, and at a `)` in a comment; a line no name list holds ends one that is not closed, and a statement in a list's comments is not read, so each line is read once), the packages above a module and the submodules a `from` names (the install walk read neither), and a module named by a literal to `importlib.import_module()` (a relative name against its package argument: a literal, `__name__`, `__package__` or `__spec__.parent`; with none, import_module raises), `__import__()` or `runpy.run_module()` (`_PY_DYNAMIC_IMPORT_RE`, tried where the names are); a name built as the code runs is not followed. `_PY_LOCAL_IMPORT_RE`, `_PY_RELATIVE_IMPORT_RE` and `_PY_REACH_MAX` retired. `ENGINE_VERSION` and the pack's `rule_set` 2.49.0 (a stored scan is scanned again). The `--deps` project scan's walk from an npm package's entry points (which files in `static/`, `public/` or `_next/` are web assets, left out of the import-time test) stopped at 5,000 files and read `exports` to 5,000 entry points: `scanner/core.py` (`_deps_npm_entries`, `_deps_npm_reach`) and the npm package's `deps.js` (`npmEntries`, `npmReach`) go to the end (each file once), and the pack's `_DEPS_REACH_MAX` is retired. Tests: `test_install_signals.py` (`ImportReachTests`: padding of 5 and 320 modules as lines, one name list and one import list; a chain of 400; the statement forms; the dynamic forms and what is not followed; the deadline in the walk; `ImportReaderTests`: a list's comments, a list not closed, linear time on seven hostile texts; `InstallReachTests`: setup.py's padding of 250, import lists, submodules, `import_module`, `src/`, a backend's own imports; 28 of the walks' cases fail on the old walks), `test_review_dependency_checks.py` and `review-dependency-checks.test.js` (a chain of 5,101 files to `static/`, 6,000 exports), `test_suppression_integrity.py` (2.49.0). Measured on the benchmark's and the popular set's 830 PyPI releases, each scanned as the guard scans it, before and after (`$SP/br5/full.py`): no verdict, finding or INCOMPLETE changes; 58 walks reach more modules, 20,244 to 40,953 in all (22 releases past 300; auth0-python 4,300, transformers 2,372); 192.5 to 205.9 s in all (+7%), the most google-cloud-aiplatform's 6.3 to 9.9 s (92 MB of Python, 1,455 modules reached, each given the import-time test), the median unchanged (0.03 s); the slowest release 14.4 s, the deadline 120. Left (the backlog's BR-5): the walk is still a reading of the text; the engine's Python parser and a resolver of its own would read what a regex cannot (an import in a string the code builds).
- **D-3: what a JavaScript script puts into a container, the container holds** (Oct 8; the post-0.1.8 backlog's D-3, "values put in a `Map` before they're sent are lost by the data flow", found wider while choosing it: on the JavaScript tree only an array's `push` and `unshift` on a name put a value into a container, so an install script that put the whole environment into a `FormData`, a `Map`, a `Set` or a `URLSearchParams`, or `Object.assign`ed it into an object, and sent that, had no finding at all: the tree answers for every text it reads, and the text follower, which reads `x.append(…)` and `x.add(…)`, only for the rest; Python's model had them, its `COLLECTS`). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/jsflow/supply.rs`: `COLLECTS` (`push`, `unshift`, `splice` from its third argument, `set`, `add`, `append`, `fill`) and `PUTS_INTO_FIRST` (`Object.assign`, `Object.defineProperty`, `Object.defineProperties`, `Reflect.set`, `Reflect.defineProperty`: their first argument), put through `sc_put_into`, which writes a name's binding (not an import's) as `push` did; one model of values, so what is received and run and what is decoded and run go into a container as what is sent does. A member's container (`o.list.push(x)`, `this.items.push(x)`) was tried (as an assignment to the member holds it, `member_write`) and left out: it joined unrelated flows in vite 8.3.2's `dist/node/chunks/node.js` (a CSS asset id decoded from hex reached es-module-lexer's `eval`, `spawn` and `module._compile`: SC-EVAL-DECODE, WARN to SUSPICIOUS) and monaco-editor 0.57.0's `vs/loader.js` (its worker's script loader: SC-USE-RISK CRITICAL, OK to SUSPICIOUS), each isolated by turning the pieces on one at a time. Rule set 2.50.0. Tests: `supply.rs` (`containers_hold_what_they_are_given`: each container and target, a module's container filled in a function, a variable in a `Headers`, what nothing sends, an import, code received into a `Map` and run), `test_supply_chain_signals.py` (`ContainerTests`, on the install and import-time tests: eight shapes, each without a finding on the old engine), `js/test/supply-chain-signals.test.js`, `test_suppression_integrity.py`. Measured: the benchmark (945) and the popular set (1,205) against the BR-5 gates', no verdict moved, and one malicious release's finding names the upload its code builds (react-milton 19.1.1: the host name and the files appended to a `FormData` and posted, CRITICAL as before, where it named a file read); the 20,422 JavaScript files of the installed npm packages here, each read by the three tree calls (`local_data_sent_tree`, `received_code_tree`, `decoded_runs_tree`), no answer changed; the 11 recorded snapshot modules unchanged. Found on the way, not changed (D-15): code received into a variable of the callback around the one that reads it, then put in a variable declared outside that callback, is not followed to the code run (`let p; https.get(u, (r) => { let d = ''; r.on('data', (c) => { d += c; }); r.on('end', () => { p = d; eval(p); }); })`); the send direction is.
- **D-15: code received in a callback and run from a variable outside it** (Oct 8; found probing D-3: `let p; https.get(u, (res) => { let d = ''; res.on('data', (c) => { d += c; }); res.on('end', () => { p = d; eval(p); }); })` had no finding on the JavaScript tree, which answers for a `.js` file, where the text detector, which answered before it, found it; the same with `p` an outer function's, or run later from a timer). The response is `https.get`'s callback's parameter: `d`, that callback's variable, holds the parameter (its key), and a shared write keeps only the keys of the functions around the variable's own (`V::within`), so the key was lost when `p = d` wrote it to a variable declared outside the callback; the summary recorded a parameter written to an outer variable only from the parameter's own function. `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/jsflow/eval.rs` (`write`): a parameter of the function writing, or of a function around it, written to a variable declared outside that function is in that function's summary (`pw_adds`, committed by the key's function), so when the callback is given the response the variable holds it. Rule set 2.51.0. Tests: `supply.rs` (`received_data_put_in_a_variable_outside_its_callback`: the module's variable, run at once and later, an outer function's; a library's request for its caller stays quiet; the send direction as before), `test_review_received_code.py` (on the tree, both tests; each fails on the old engine), `js/test/review-received-code.test.js`, `test_suppression_integrity.py`. Measured against D-3's gates: the benchmark (945) and the popular set (1,205) identical; the 20,422 JavaScript files of the installed npm packages, the three tree calls unchanged; the 11 recorded snapshot modules unchanged.
- **D-12: a package manager's install at import time, of a package the release does not depend on** (Oct 8; the backlog's D-12, from D-1's reading of the in-sample misses: crypto-hash-sdk 1.0.1 ran `npm uninstall prettier-sdk && npm install prettier-sdk` when it was imported, hidden (no output, no window, any error swallowed), and was WARN). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/signs.rs`: `import_time_risk_with` is `import_time_risk` given the release's declared names (the API's `import_time_risk` call takes them as `declared`; without them nothing changes), and adds `undeclared_installs`: the command lines a text hands an exec call (`shell::exec_command_lines`) and a program with its arguments as a list (`_PM_ARGV_RE`: `spawn('npm', ['i', 'x'])`, `subprocess.check_call([sys.executable, '-m', 'pip', 'install', 'x'])`; `sys.executable` read as python, `process.execPath` as node, any other item as a name the code builds), each read as a shell reads it (`installed_packages`: npm's, pnpm's, yarn's, bun's and cnpm's `install`, `i` and `add` and npm's other spellings of install, `yarn global add`; pip's `install` run as pip, pip3, `python -m pip` or `uv pip`; an option that takes a value skipped with its value). A package counts when the manifest does not name it (npm's names compared in lower case, pip's as PEP 503 normalizes them), and not when it is a local path, an archive or `file:`, a name the code builds (a variable, an f-string's or a template's hole) or a package manager or build tool installing itself (`_PM_SELF`); a URL or a git source counts as written. In Python a statement in a function's body runs when the module is loaded only where the module runs that function when it is loaded, and not in its `if __name__ == "__main__":` block (its `else:` does): `PyLoad`, read from the tokens (pyparse's tokenizer: a string's lines, a comment and the lines inside brackets are not the module's lines), finds each function's body and the `__main__` block, and a function of the module's own (not a method nor a function within a function) runs when the module refers to it where it runs when loaded (a call, `atexit.register(f)`, `Thread(target=f)`) or `@atexit.register` decorates it; a text the tokenizer refuses is read by indentation (`py_runs_at_load`, a line a closing bracket starts skipped). litellm 1.103.0, the benchmark's benign one, installs supabase, sentry_sdk or slack_bolt when one of its integrations is set up and the package is missing, and was SUSPICIOUS before the rule; read by indentation alone, onnxruntime's whisper helper (installed here) was one, its `datasets` installed in a static method whose signature closes with `):` at the method's indentation, and a string's lines could hide a module's statement (`'''` then `def fake():` at the left edge). The decoded view is read too, as for the other reasons. The reason, "installs packages the release does not depend on (names)", is a strong one (`_STRONG_IMPORT_REASONS`, core's and the pack's): SC-IMPORT-RISK CRITICAL. `registry/repo.py`: `_declared_names` (npm: package.json's name and the names of its dependencies, devDependencies, optionalDependencies, peerDependencies and bundled dependencies; a wheel's METADATA or an sdist's PKG-INFO: Name and every Requires-Dist, an extra's too; an sdist whose PKG-INFO names no dependency, whose setup.py may, and any other artifact: not known, so not judged), given to the import-time test alone (an install script fetches what its package needs, as @swc/core's postinstall installs @swc/wasm when its native binding fails, and a command line tool installs plugins when it is used); the memo's key holds the list's hash. `scanner/engine.py`'s `import_time_risks` passes `declared`. The guard scans as the registry does (`_scan_artifact`). `ENGINE_VERSION` and the pack's `rule_set` 2.52.0. Tests: `signs.rs` (`package_install_tests`: the packages command lines install, and what they do not; the crypto-hash-sdk shape, declared names, both argument lists, a package manager itself, a name built, text that is only text, an f-string's hole, a function the module calls, hands on or `@atexit.register` decorates and one it does not, a body on its header's line, a `try` block, a method and a multi-line signature, a docstring at the left edge, a string's lines, a `__main__` block and its `else:`, a text the tokenizer refuses), `tests/registry/test_import_installs.py` (npm: the crypto-hash-sdk shape CRITICAL and SUSPICIOUS, each kind of dependency declared, a postinstall and a file nothing loads at import; PyPI: a wheel, the name declared under another spelling and by an extra, an install in a function the module does not run and in one it runs or hands to `atexit.register`, an sdist with and without Requires-Dist; 4 of the 6 fail on the old engine), `test_suppression_integrity.py`. Measured against D-15's gates: the benchmark (945), one verdict moved, crypto-hash-sdk 1.0.1 WARN to SUSPICIOUS (its index.js, prettier-sdk), no other finding changed; the popular set (1,205) identical; the 11 recorded snapshot modules unchanged; with every install counted (an empty manifest), the import-time test on the 30,241 Python and JavaScript files installed here (2,252 hold a package manager's name) finds none. Left: a `--deps` scan (Python's and the npm package's), whose installed packages carry their manifests too.
- **D-13: a package the code loads when the package is loaded, that package.json names only in devDependencies** (Oct 8; the backlog's D-13, from D-1's reading of the in-sample misses: dotenv-express 17.4.3, a copy of dotenv, requires `environment-gate` at the top of `lib/main.js` and calls it first in `config()`, and was OK; the row read "a `require()` of a package the manifest doesn't declare", and reading the release showed it is declared, as a devDependency, which npm does not install for the package's users). Measured first, with a survey on the engine's tree (`$SP/d13/survey2.py`: what the files the entry points reach load when they run, against package.json): of the popular set's 801 npm releases, the benchmark's 219 popular ones and the 1,623 npm packages installed here, a devDependency loaded at load time is always one of npm's 5,000 most-downloaded packages (@testing-library/jest-dom's `@jest/globals`, es-abstract's `for-each`, typed-array-byte-length's `available-typed-arrays`, escodegen's `optionator`); a package no field names is a framework's adapter or a module the host provides (cypress's `cypress/svelte` loads svelte, which is not one of the 5,000; and in the benchmark's compromised libraries' own code, ngx-toastr's `@angular/animations` and Ember addons' `@ember/*`), so that half is left out. `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/jsloads.rs` (new): `js_loads(text, path)` reads the module on `js_parse`'s tree and gives each specifier it loads when it runs, its line and how: a `require()` given a literal (a string, or a template without substitutions) outside any function, class body (a static block and static fields run when the class is defined) and `try` statement; an import or export-from declaration (in TypeScript, not one of types alone); `import x = require()`; an `import()` given a literal outside any function; and `npm_package`, the package a specifier names (none for a path, a `#` import, a URL or another scheme, `node:`, `bun:`, or one of Node's built-in modules). The API's `js_loads` call (`{"path": …}` for the dialect) answers `[[specifier, line, how, package]]`, or null when the module does not parse, on the engine's own stack. `registry/repo.py`: `_dev_only_loads`, a phase after the dependencies nothing uses, for npm: the packages package.json names only in devDependencies (not in dependencies, optionalDependencies, peerDependencies or the bundled lists), less npm's 5,000 most-downloaded (by the registry name an alias installs), the release's own scope and Ember's own modules (`@ember/*`, `@glimmer/*`: the Ember app provides them, so an addon lists them for its tests); the modules are what the main entry and the commands reach (`_npm_main_targets`: main, or index.js; exports' "." entry under every condition but types; bin; then `_reach_of`), not a subpath export, which runs when a user imports it by name (@dev-blinq/cucumber-js 1.0.131, a fork of @cucumber/cucumber, exports `./lib/*`, and its `lib/api/test_helpers.js` loads its test tools), nor a type declaration (`.d.ts`, `.d.cts`, `.d.mts`: the exports' types conditions reach them); a module is read only when it quotes such a name (`unused_deps.quoted_in`), through the content memo (`_js_loads`). SC-DEV-DEPENDENCY (MAJOR, so WARN; CWE-829), at the first load, naming the packages. `ENGINE_VERSION` and the pack's `rule_set` 2.53.0. Tests: `jsloads.rs` (`what_a_module_loads_when_it_runs`: dotenv-express's shape, each form, a condition, what runs later (a function, an arrow, a method, an instance field, a `try`), a static block and field, what the code builds, TypeScript's forms, what does not parse; `the_package_a_specifier_names`), `tests/registry/test_dev_dependency_loads.py` (dotenv-express's shape WARN; the forms; a module the entry loads; exports' "." entry, conditions and a command; not: what runs later, what the release installs, a popular package, its own scope, Ember's, a subpath export, a type declaration, a file nothing loads; the four tests of what is found fail on the old engine), `test_suppression_integrity.py`. Measured against D-12's gates (g40): the benchmark (945), one verdict moved, dotenv-express 17.4.3 OK to WARN, no other finding changed (reading every module the entry points reach, three compromised releases already SUSPICIOUS gained one: @dev-blinq/cucumber-js's exported test helper and two type declarations, which the main entry rule leaves out); the popular set (1,205) identical; the 11 recorded snapshot modules unchanged.
- **D-2: a download a JavaScript callback is given, written to a file and run** (Oct 8; the post-0.1.8 backlog's D-2, "a file written, then run, when the data comes in as a parameter", with the probe D-1 added: `request.get(url, (e, r, b) => { fs.writeFileSync(p, b); exec('python3 ' + p) })`). Probed on D-13's engine: the request client's callback, https.get's response gathered in `data` and written in `end`, one piped into `fs.createWriteStream(p)` and run on `finish`, and a helper given the data all had no finding; only a download awaited in the same function was found. `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/jsflow/`: `Fn::param_files` (mod.rs), a function's summary of the files a parameter is written to (the path's keys, where), apart from the eight categories' 8-bit mask, which is full; a reading's additions (`Eval::pf_adds`, eval.rs) are committed as `param_writes`' are, marking the parameter changed; `sc_file_write` (supply.rs) adds one for a parameter written by `writeFileSync`, a stream's `write`/`end` or a pipe, and the call's summary application (`apply`) gives each such parameter what the call gives it (`sc_param_file`): a download, or code the script decodes or carves out, is a written file (`record_written`), and a parameter of the caller's is carried to the caller's summary (a helper's to the callback that calls it). The write is known only when the client gives the callback the download, after the callback that runs the file was read, so `sc_file_run` keeps a run whose file no write is known for yet (`Supply::runs`, `PendingRun`), and `facts_here` matches them against what is written at the end (`late_drops`, through `run_of_written` and `dropped_out`, which `sc_file_run` and `sc_dropped` now use too; a run already reported is not again). Rule set 2.54.0. Tests: `supply.rs` (`a_download_a_callback_is_given_written_then_run`: the four shapes, found as "downloads a script and runs it with Python"; a program downloaded and run, a download kept and not run, and what a read of the package's own file gives a callback, not), `tests/registry/test_droppers.py` (`CallbackTests`: the four in an install script, SUSPICIOUS, and one at import time, CRITICAL; the installer's and the kept download, nothing; both found-tests fail on the old engine), `js/test/supply-chain-signals.test.js`, `test_suppression_integrity.py`. Measured against D-13's runs: the benchmark (945) and the popular set (1,205) identical; the 20,422 JavaScript files of the installed npm packages, the four tree calls (`local_data_sent_tree`, `received_code_tree`, `decoded_runs_tree`, `dropped_run_tree`) unchanged.
- **B-6: a Python comprehension over the environment reads the lists its test names** (Oct 8; the post-0.1.8 backlog's B-6, probed on D-2's engine: `{k: v for k, v in os.environ.items() if any(p in k for p in PATTERNS)}` with `PATTERNS = ['TOKEN', 'SECRET']` and sent had no finding, read as a selection of variables; with the words in the test it was the whole environment; the JavaScript model already read a named list, `sc_selects`). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/pyflow/supply.rs`: `sc_selects_env` (now `&mut self`) reads, past the tests' own text, what the names they read (`own_nodes`' `Name`s, at most eight) are given in the function or in the nearest function or module around it that gives them (`sc_index`'s assignments, the first 4,000 characters of each, at most eight): a secret word there (`_SH_SECRET_VAR_RE`) makes the comprehension the whole environment, as one in the test does. A test of named prefixes (`PREFIXES = ('VITE_', 'APP_')` and `k.startswith(PREFIXES)`) still selects. Rule set 2.55.0. Tests: `pyflow::supply::tests::the_environment_by_variable_and_whole` (the module's list, a function's tuple, the prefixes), `test_supply_chain_signals.py` (`NamedListTests`: both shapes in install scripts and at import time, the prefixes quiet; the found test fails on the old engine), `test_suppression_integrity.py`. Measured against D-2's runs: the benchmark (945) and the popular set (1,205) identical (the selections in vite, kubernetes and future stay quiet); the 9,819 Python files of the installed packages (`/usr/lib/python3/dist-packages`, `/usr/local/lib/python3.11/dist-packages`), the four tree calls unchanged.
- **D-9: code written into another package's folder** (Oct 8; the backlog's D-9, from D-1's reading of the in-sample misses: @dinzid04/libsignal-node 2.2.5 finds @whiskeysockets/baileys, by `require.resolve` of its package.json or a path joined from `node_modules`, and, a second after it is loaded, writes its own text over baileys's `lib/Socket/newsletter.js`; it was OK). Measured first, with a text survey (`$SP/d9/survey.py`: a JavaScript file that writes a file and names another package's folder): in the benchmark, the popular set and the npm packages installed here such files are tests (pino's, resolve's), a tool writing in its own package (esbuild's install script), resolvers' lookups (get-tsconfig's, jiti's), bundles naming the packages they inline (prettier's CLI) and, in the benchmark's compromised libraries, packages of their own scope; so a rewrite is a code file written into a package that is neither the release's own nor of its scope. `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/jsflow/supply.rs`: the model's values have one more kind, a path in another package's folder (`K_PKG`, what: the package): `require.resolve('<pkg>…')` of Node's `require` or of one `createRequire()` made, and `import.meta.resolve` (`sc_require_resolve`: the model's descriptions name no member of `require`), and a path joined (`path.join`, `path.resolve`, their `posix` and `win32` forms) from `node_modules` and a package's name among its literal parts (`sc_pkg_path`; a dot folder such as `.cache` is no package), carried as values are; a code file (a literal ending in `.js`, `.cjs` or `.mjs` in the path's text or in what the name it is given holds, `sc_code_path`) written there (`writeFileSync`, `writeFile`, `appendFile*`, `createWriteStream`) or copied or moved there (`copyFile*`, `rename*`, `cp*`, their second argument) is `Out::Rewrote` (driver.rs), listed by the facts (`Facts::rewrote`, `supply::rewrote`). `signs.rs`: `rewrite_reasons` gives each package "rewrites another package's code (the package)" (`_REWRITE_REASON`, a strong reason: core's and the pack's `_STRONG_IMPORT_REASONS`), at most three, in the install-script test and the import-time test (SC-INSTALL-HOOK and SC-IMPORT-RISK, CRITICAL); `own_release` names the release for what a call reads, a command's inline code too (the API's `install_script_risk` and `import_time_risk` take `own`, the release's name): its own package, or one of its scope, is its own code. `scanner/engine.py` passes `own`; `registry/repo.py` gives it the npm release's name (`_own_name`, package.json's) wherever the two tests run, and the import-time memo's key holds it. `ENGINE_VERSION` and the pack's `rule_set` 2.56.0. Tests: `supply.rs` (`code_written_in_another_packages_folder`: libsignal-node's shape, `require.resolve` alone, a file copied over one, `node_modules/<pkg>` in one part, an ES module's `createRequire()` and `import.meta.resolve`; not a data file, a dot folder, a read, the script's own folder, a `require` of the script's own), `signs.rs` (`rewrite_tests`: both tests, JavaScript alone, the release's own name and scope, the name put back), `tests/registry/test_rewrites.py` (libsignal-node's shape at import, CRITICAL and SUSPICIOUS, and in an install script; each way to name the folder; the release's own package or scope; a data file, a dot folder, a read; the three tests of what is found fail on the old engine), `js/test/supply-chain-signals.test.js`, `test_suppression_integrity.py`. Measured against B-6's runs: the benchmark (945), one verdict moved, @dinzid04/libsignal-node 2.2.5 OK to SUSPICIOUS, no other finding changed; the popular set (1,205) identical; the import-time test on the 20,422 JavaScript files of the installed npm packages (the 335 that name `node_modules`, `require.resolve` or `import.meta.resolve`) finds no rewrite; nor do 43 npm packages that generate, patch or install code (`$SP/d9f/extra.sh`: prisma, @prisma/client, patch-package, husky, puppeteer, electron, node-gyp, next, react-native, expo, nx, lerna, npm, pnpm, yarn, corepack, sharp, bcrypt, sqlite3 among them), whose verdicts are those of the first build (prisma 8.0.0-rc.21, lerna 10.0.1 and corepack 0.36.0 are SUSPICIOUS for other reasons, as they were on the engine before Oct 8's work: the backlog's D-16 to D-18). Left: a path that climbs out of the package's own folder to a sibling's (`path.join(__dirname, '..', '<pkg>')`, D-9b); a `--deps` scan does not give the tests each installed package's name, so a dependency's write into its own folder by its own name would read as another's (none of the 20,422 installed files does so: D-9c).
- **D-16 and D-18: prisma and corepack, SUSPICIOUS for what they read of the environment and require** (Oct 8; found measuring D-9 on 43 npm packages that generate, patch or install code: prisma 8.0.0-rc.21 and corepack 0.36.0 were SUSPICIOUS, as on the engine before Oct 8's work). prisma's `getApiBaseUrl(env = process.env)` reads `env.PRISMA_MANAGEMENT_API_URL`; shown on small texts, process.env held by a parameter (its default too), an object's member or a copy (`{ ...process.env }`, `Object.assign({}, process.env)`) keeps no mark (`V::plain` drops `OBJ_ENV`), and an object's members are read as the whole object, so the member was the whole environment, and the Management API's answers it reached were sent in an address (SC-IMPORT-RISK CRITICAL); corepack's env file gives `{ env: { …parsed, ...process.env }, path }` and the caller's `localEnv?.path` read the same way (found by taking each `process.env` out in turn). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/jsflow/`: `sc_member` (supply.rs) reads a member of a value holding the whole environment and no mark, or of a parameter, by its name: a variable's (`env_var_name`: capitals, digits and underscores, `npm_*`, the proxies') is that variable, one with `env` in it (`env_holder_name`) is all of it, any other is not the environment; a parameter read so, or given to a method of its own (`sc_call`: not one of `PASS_THROUGH`, the built-in methods that give back what the object holds), holds its key with `MEMBER_KEY` set (mod.rs: `V::through_member`, `key_owner`, `key_index`, which every reading of a key now uses), and the summary keeps whether the function returns (`ret_params`' third field) or sends (`reach_member`) the parameter only so, in which case a call gives it its argument without the whole environment (`sc_through_member`; eval.rs's `commit` and `apply`). With that, prisma's telemetry sender, which builds its event from `buildTelemetryEvent(payload, config, env)` given `{ env: process.env, … }`, was the next SUSPICIOUS reason, and is not one either. D-18: corepack also requires the package.json of the package manager it downloaded, `require(path.join(tmpFolder, 'package.json'))`, read as loading a module named by data it received; require parses a JSON file and runs none of it, so a `require` whose argument ends in a literal ending in `.json` is no module load (`sc_json_module`). Still the whole environment: the copy or the holder sent whole, the holder's `env`, a parameter returned or sent whole or beside a member of it; no longer: a holder under another name read by that name and sent (`{ data: process.env }`, then `payload.data`). `ENGINE_VERSION` and the pack's `rule_set` 2.57.0. Tests: `supply.rs` (`copies_and_holders_of_the_environment_read_by_name`, `a_json_file_given_require_is_parsed`), `tests/registry/test_env_copies.py` (prisma's shapes, its telemetry's, the copies and holders, corepack's require: OK; the copy, the holder, its `env`, the parameter returned whole and a module named by received data: still found; the four tests of what is no longer found fail on the old engine), `js/test/supply-chain-signals.test.js`, `test_suppression_integrity.py`. Measured against D-9's gates (g44): the benchmark (945) and the popular set (1,205) identical; the 20,422 JavaScript files of the installed npm packages, the four tree calls unchanged but Node's own corepack (`/opt/node22/lib/node_modules/corepack`: its send is COREPACK_NPM_TOKEN, not the whole environment, and its require no load); of the 43 packages D-9 was measured on, prisma 8.0.0-rc.21 and corepack 0.36.0 SUSPICIOUS to OK and no other verdict moved. lerna 10.0.1 (D-17) stays SUSPICIOUS: the cross-file follower reads a function's body with its strings blanked, so `exec("git", …)` names no program there.
- **tiny_https's drop of Oct 8** (Oct 8; John: "Updated tiny https."; `tiny_https-2026-10-08l.tgz`, SHA-256 `d94f7c43…`, 579 files). Taken with `scripts/sync_tiny_https.py`, which takes `roots/` too now: `src/tuf.rs` builds in `roots/sigstore_tuf_root.json` (Sigstore's TUF root), and `roots/mozilla.pem` is Mozilla's root store for the `mozilla-roots` feature, which Lazaret does not turn on. The sdist ships the TUF root and not the PEM (`_build/lazaret_build.py`'s `RUST_VENDORED`; `scripts/check_native_library.py` checks an sdist has it; `test_build_backend.py`, `test_sync_tiny_https.py`). In the drop: B-48 (`http::connect`: the host name looked up on a thread of its own, so `connect_timeout` bounds a slow resolver, the answer kept 30 seconds, the addresses raced as RFC 8305 says; the blocking client Lazaret uses connects through it), B-74 (a request scheduler: in flight per host, a byte budget, batches under a deadline, cancelled together), B-61 (timers for async code), B-35 and B-47 (TLS 1.3 session resumption), B-39 (a TLS stream split for two threads), B-63 to B-65 (OCSP responders asked, the whole chain's revocation, real CAs' answers replayed), B-81 (certificate transparency for Fulcio's certificates), B-82 (Sigstore's trusted root through TUF), B-31 and B-101 (Mozilla's root store, the operating systems' own), B-33 and B-34 (ECDSA P-521, RSA-PSS, internationalized names) and speed (B-49, B-57, B-62, B-85, B-87, B-89, B-90). No interface Lazaret uses changed: lazaret-net, lazaret-verify and lazaret-ffi build and pass as they were (46 tests), and so do the Python package's network tests (nativenet, the guard's native fetches, provenance, the Go checksum database, the sources, pmsettings). CI and the gates run the library's new offline tests (`tuf_sigstore`, `tuf_synthetic`, `wycheproof`, `real_revocation`) with its others (1,476 pass, 21 ignored). One new unit test, `http::schedule_tests::a_cancel_stops_waiting_and_running_requests_and_fails_new_ones`, is skipped there: it starts the slow request on a thread and expects it to take one of the scheduler's two places before the third request does, which a busy machine does not always keep (2 failures in 7 runs here); the fix, waiting for `/slow` to reach the server before the other two start, is the library's, asked for its next drop.
- **D-17: a program named in another file, given the data as its arguments** (Oct 8; found measuring D-9: lerna 10.0.1 was SUSPICIOUS, as on the engine before Oct 8's work). `dist/chunk-EB6RZPL6.js:725` calls `gitCheckout(dirtyManifests, gitOpts, this.execOpts)`, and `gitCheckout` (`dist/chunk-WC2B4V4E.js`) runs `exec("git", ["checkout", "--"].concat(files), execOpts)`, lerna's wrapper of execa. Taking `exec(` out of gitCheckout's body cleared it; a two-file package of that shape was SUSPICIOUS whether the call was on one line or several. The cross-file follower read each function's body with its strings blanked, to find what the module defines, imports and exports, and its received-code test read the same rows, `exec("   ", …)`. `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/crossfile.rs`: a JavaScript body is kept as written beside its blanked rows (`Module::code_of`: the same spans of the code rows, found by where each blanked row is while the module is parsed; Python's bodies stay as they were), and `direct_runners` and `runners` read that. `received.rs`: `fixed_command`: a call of exec's (`exec`, `execSync`, `child_process.exec`) whose first argument is a literal, plain command line (no `;`, `|`, `&`, `$`, `%`, `!`, `^`, `=`, globs, brackets, quotes or redirection: not Python's `exec("import os; …")` either), none of whose words is a program that runs what it is given, runs none of the data after it: not an interpreter, a shell or an opener (`_DL_RUNNERS`), a wrapper (`_HOOK_WRAPPERS`: `env`, `sudo`, `nice` …) or a program that runs the command its arguments name (the pack's new `_CMD_RUNNERS`: `xargs`, `timeout`, `setsid`, `npx`, `su`, `wsl`, `rundll32` …); a `.` past the first word is a path. Still found: the data as the program, and an interpreter or such a program given it (`exec("node", ["-e", data])`, `exec("timeout", ["5"].concat(data))`). `ENGINE_VERSION` and the pack's `rule_set` 2.58.0. Tests: `received.rs` (`a_program_named_by_a_literal_runs_no_data`: lerna's body on one line and several, a fixed command line, a command line that starts with a fixed program (not a run before either); the data as the program, an interpreter given it; what is no fixed program: an interpreter through `env`, `sudo node`, `sh -c`, `. ./env.sh`, `xargs`, `timeout 5 node`, `npx`, `%COMSPEC%`, a glob, `eval`, Python's code; the data given to `timeout`), `tests/registry/test_fixed_programs.py` (lerna's shape across two files, OK; the data as the program, `node -e` and `timeout`, SUSPICIOUS; the three cases of lerna's shape fail on the old engine), `js/test/crossfile.test.js` (the same shapes through the npm package's follower), `test_suppression_integrity.py`; the 11 recorded snapshot modules unchanged. Measured against D-16's runs (g45): the benchmark (945) and the popular set (1,205) identical; the 43 packages D-9 was measured on, as an offline set now (`$SP/partf/extra43.jsonl`, each tarball checked against the registry's integrity), lerna SUSPICIOUS to OK and nothing else moved (30 OK, 13 WARN).
- **D-3b: a member's container** (Oct 8; the backlog's D-3b, which D-3 left: `o.list.push(x)`, `this.items.push(x)` and `Object.assign(this.opts, …)` were not followed on the JavaScript tree, since put into the object that holds the member, as an assignment to a name's member is, what they were given reached every member read of that object). First, which part joined flows: D-3's attempt made vite 8.3.2 and monaco-editor 0.57.0 SUSPICIOUS; built both ways and run on those two (`$SP/d3b/variant.sh`), a name's member held apart from the object left both as they were, and a member of `this` made monaco-editor's `vs/loader.js` SC-USE-RISK CRITICAL: its LoaderEventRecorder's `record(r, c) { this._events.push(new LoaderEvent(r, c, now)) }` held what every caller recorded, a plugin's `load(…)` read as a download among it, and that reached its check that eval runs, `self.eval(policy.createScript('', 'true'))` (found by taking each `this.x.push` out of the file in turn, then the received value's first reads). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/jsflow/supply.rs`: `sc_member_container`, a member as a container of its own: `this.items` (`sc_this_member`, a binding already) and `o.list`, a name's static member (not an import's), a binding beside `o`'s, held by the function that holds `o` and shared as `o` is (`sc_props`' key 5); `sc_put_into` puts a container's `push`, `unshift`, `splice`, `set`, `add`, `append` or `fill`, and `Object.assign`'s and the others' target, into it when it is a member, and into a member of `this` without what the function holds of its own parameters (`V::within` of none), so a method's argument is not put there from every caller; `sc_member_held` (eval.rs's `member`) reads it with `o.list`, for the names of members the module puts anything into as written (`collected_members`, once per module, `Program::sc_collected`). Not followed: the object sent whole after a member's container is filled (`JSON.stringify(o)`), a parameter's member's container (the caller's object), and what a method is given put into a member of `this` (the recorder). `ENGINE_VERSION` and the pack's `rule_set` 2.59.0. Tests: `supply.rs` (`a_members_container_holds_what_it_is_given`: a name's list, Map and `Object.assign` target, a module's object filled in one function and sent in another declared before it, `this.items` and `this.opts`, code received into a member's container and run; not the object's other members, not a recorder's events), `tests/scanner/test_supply_chain_signals.py` (`MemberContainerTests`: six shapes, each without a finding on the old engine; the object's other members), `js/test/supply-chain-signals.test.js`, `test_suppression_integrity.py`. Measured against D-17's gates (g47): the benchmark (945), the popular set (1,205), Part F's sets and `extra43` identical; the 20,422 JavaScript files of the installed npm packages, the four tree calls: no answer changed (`$SP/d3bm`); the 11 recorded snapshot modules unchanged.
