- **D-12b and D-9c: a `--deps` scan gives the tests each installed package's names** (Oct 8; the backlog's D-12b and D-9c, left in D-12 and D-9: a `--deps` scan's import-time test was given no names, so an install at import of a package the release does not depend on was judged in registry and guard scans and not in an installed tree, and a package's code written into its own folder by its own name would read as another package's). `python/src/lazaret/scanner/core.py`: `_InstalledNames` (`of(path)`: (declared, own) or None) gives an npm package's names from its package.json, the nearest `node_modules/<name>` or `node_modules/@scope/<name>` of the file's path (`_is_package_root`), its own name and the packages it names of every kind (`npm_declared_names`, now the registry's too), and its name as `own`; and a Python distribution's from the `*.dist-info` whose RECORD lists the file's top-level module or package under a site-packages or dist-packages directory (`_xf_record_tops`), its METADATA's Name and Requires-Dist (`metadata_declared_names`, now the registry's too; several that list one, all of theirs); `dependency_checks` gives each file's to the import-time test (`engine.import_time_risks`' `names`, per item) and the package's own name to its install scripts' test (`_follow_dependency_hook`). `registry/repo.py`'s `_declared_names` reads through the two shared functions. The npm package's twin: `js/src/deps.js` (`InstalledNames`, `npmDeclaredNames`, `metadataDeclaredNames`; a worker's task carries the file's names) and `lib/native.js` (`importTimeRisk`'s `names`, `installScriptRisk`'s `own`). No engine change. Tests: `tests/scanner/test_deps_package_names.py` (an install at import of what an installed package does not declare, scoped and nested too; what it declares, of each kind; no package.json; its own folder at import and in its install script, and another's; a distribution's install at import of what it does not require, what it requires, no `.dist-info`; the shared readings: the found cases and the own-folder cases fail on the old code), `js/test/review-dependency-checks.test.js`, `tests/architecture/test_js_parity.py` (unchanged, passing). - **B-3: a method reads `this` as the instance it is called on, in a class made in several places** (Oct 8; the backlog's B-3, from B-1). B-1 made each instance of a class made in several places (`new C(…)` of it or a subclass in more than one place: vite's MagicString) a container of its own: what a method is given from a call on an instance is put into the instance's variable, not the class's `this.x` (`apply` skips such a parameter write; `sc_call`'s `sc_instance_call`), so `r.setCode(t); r.run()`, where `run` evals `this.c`, ran nothing received: `run`'s `this.c` held nothing a call gave. `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/jsflow/`: in a method of such a class, `this` is the method's receiver, a parameter of index `RECV_INDEX` (mod.rs; `supply::sc_this_value`, eval.rs's `this`), and `this.x` reads the class's binding and the receiver (`sc_member`); a call of a method of such a class gives the receiver the value it is called on, for the sinks the method reaches through `this` (`sc_call` sets `Eval::recv_val`, which `apply` takes: `bound_or_recv` in the reach loop; what the method returns or keeps of `this` stays with B-1's instance, which already gives back what it holds), so what the instance's methods were given reaches what `run` does with `this.c`, through another method of its own too (`this.run()` gives `run` the caller's receiver), and another instance's call reaches nothing. A class made once is read as before. `ENGINE_VERSION` and the pack's `rule_set` 2.60.0. Tests: `supply.rs` (`a_method_reads_this_as_its_receiver`: received code given to one method and run by another, directly and through a method of its own, and not by another instance; the same for what is sent), `tests/scanner/test_supply_chain_signals.py` (`InstanceTests`: the two found cases fail on the old engine), `js/test/supply-chain-signals.test.js`, `test_suppression_integrity.py`. The first build gave the receiver to what a method returns and keeps too: in pdfjs-dist 5.7.284's `build/pdf.worker.min.mjs` (installed here, in no gate set) that joined a value its model reads as received, through the returns of many methods of its widely made classes (`getRaw`, `getBaseStreams` …), with the `wasmUrl` its JBig2 decoder's fallback imports (an `import()` of `this.#I` followed by `jbig2_nowasm_fallback.js`): a new "loads a module named by data it receives" (traced by printing each tainted receiver a call gave). - **BR-4, part 1: npm's reading of a tarball beside the registry's** (Oct 8; the backlog's BR-4, from the efficiency and brittleness review: archive headers and names read through CPython's tarfile and zipfile; its first step, a differential fuzzer against the installers' own readers). `scripts/fuzz`'s new target `archive-npm-diff` runs npm's own node-tar (the one npm bundles, beside the node on PATH; `npm_extract.cjs`: pacote's options, the first path part stripped, links dropped) on each input beside `repo.iter_archive(…, "npm")`, half the inputs with every header's checksum made right, from seeds and from tarballs built of the parts the readers disagree on. Its first runs found npm writing what the scan did not read (F-10 in `docs/0.1.9-findings.md`: a ustar prefix that starts with NUL and whose 131st byte is not, read by node-tar as `/index.js`, which npm writes as the package's main file, and by tarfile as a top-level `index.js`, which the registry dropped; a pax global header's `path`; a regular file named as a directory; a file header with a link name; a pax value with a newline; `N` and `X` headers; a name that is not UTF-8; a NUL before a newline; roots, which node-tar takes off by Windows' rules on every platform). `registry/npmtar.py` models node-tar 7.5's reading (`NodeTar`: the header's decode with the pax and long-name headers in force, JavaScript's `parseInt`, `trim`, `/\0.*/` and UTF-8 with U+FFFD, the meta entries and their 1 MiB limit, two null blocks as the end, a truncated entry; `written_path`: Unpack's strip, roots and `..`); `_iter_tar` feeds it the stream tarfile reads (`_Tee`) and `npmtar.disagreements` compares, by where each entry's data starts: an entry npm writes that tarfile read at another place, under another path or not at all, a header node-tar finds invalid, or a size node-tar would read by JavaScript's arithmetic, makes the archive corrupt (INCOMPLETE); `canonical_member_path` takes a drive-relative root off for npm as node-tar does (`C:index.js` is index.js). The target also holds the model to npm's node-tar (what `NodeTar` says npm writes is what npm writes). `ENGINE_VERSION` and the pack's `rule_set` 2.63.0. Tests: `tests/registry/test_npm_tar_readers.py` (each case, with npm's node-tar run on it where node is here; tarballs written the usual ways and by node-tar read the same in both; the model's readings), `tests/architecture/test_fuzz_scripts.py` (the target's promises), `test_suppression_integrity.py`. Next in BR-4: pip's reading of an sdist on the Python that installs it, cargo's, VS Code's zip reader; then the headers in Rust. Measured against BR-2's gates (g52): the benchmark (945), the popular set (1,205), Part F's sets and the 43 packages of extra43 identical; the 1,361 npm tarballs of the benchmark, the popular set and extra43 read the same by both (no corrupt member, the same files); reading an npm tarball about a fifth longer (10.3 s to 12.7 s for those 1,361; next 16.4.0, 199 MB unpacked, 0.24 s more), a small part of a scan; the fuzz target about 100,000 inputs over the day, nothing left; 0.1.8 as released reads only package.json from the prefix case. The 11 recorded snapshot modules unchanged.

- **H-2: `lazaret hook` in the npm package, and a review of H-1** (Oct 9; the backlog's H-2: the npm package pointed to the Python package for the commit-time gate). `js/src/hook.js` is `lazaret.scanner.hook`'s twin: the same files checked (the staged files, or the files named; their staged content from git's index, a file git doesn't track from disk), the same scan, the same gate and the same lines printed, with the same exit codes; `cli.js` dispatches `lazaret hook` to it as `lazaret._cli` does. It runs only git (`rev-parse`, `diff --cached`, `ls-files`, `cat-file --batch`) and only the git `PATH` names by an absolute path (`js/src/lib/programs.js`, `lazaret.scanner.programs`' twin: libuv, like CreateProcess, looks in the working folder first on Windows, and an empty or relative `PATH` entry is the current folder everywhere), streams the blobs to disk as they come, and keeps a path's bytes as git gives them. The npm CLI's project scan is one function now (`js/src/project.js`, `collectProject` and `scanCollected`, as `core.scan_project` is), which `lazaret <dir>` and the hook both run. Writing the twin, H-1 was read again, and four things were wrong in it: (1) a file committed in a dependency's folder (`node_modules`, a virtualenv, a `vendor` folder with its marker) went unchecked, since the hook scanned the commit as `lazaret <dir>` does, which leaves those folders out without `--deps`: both hooks scan as `--deps` does now (a key in `node_modules/x/settings.py` passed the gate); (2) two names a commit holds could be one file in the hook's temporary tree, the second written over the first, which went unread: `a\b.py` over `a/b.py` on Linux (a backslash ends a folder's name there, as on Windows) and `a.py` over `A.py` on macOS and Windows (a key in `a/b.py` passed with `a\b.py` staged beside it): each file is created new now, and one whose name is taken is SC-TRUNCATED ("its name is another file's on this system …"), which fails the gate; (3) when git stopped before printing every blob, the files after the first it did not print were neither written nor reported (the gate failed on that one all the same), and a blob-mode index entry that git prints as a tree put the next file's reading out of step: each blob it did not print is reported now, and another object's bytes are skipped; (4) paths printed with the system's separator from the scan and git's from the files not copied: both print git's form now, and the not-copied reasons no longer carry the temporary folder's path. Tests: `js/test/hook.test.js` (21: H-1's cases, the four above, the stand-in git that stops, a repository's own `git` and a `PATH` of `""` and `.` before git's folder, the options, the dispatch, `programs.js`), `tests/scanner/test_hook.py` (five new cases; on H-1's hook the dependency folders, the backslash pair, the tree entry and the git that stops fail, and the case pair too where names ignore case), `tests/architecture/test_js_parity_hook.py` (both commands on eight staged trees, staged and named and `-q`: the same output and exit code; CI's `js` job names it), `js/test/cli.test.js` (`lazaret hook` outside a repository). Measured: the full gates (g57) against the pratique commit's (g56): the benchmark (945), the popular set (1,205), Part F's sets and extra43 identical; the 11 recorded snapshot modules, Lazaret's cargo tests (432), the WebAssembly check and build, the npm package's tests (84 files), the Python suite module by module (the two guard modules past the 45 s box pass class by class) and in one process (5,663 tests), the touched modules on 3.10 and 3.13 (40) and the fuzz targets pass, but for one line of the new tests that `test_portability` caught (a text-mode `open()` without an encoding, for the empty probe file; opened in binary now, that module and the touched modules on 3.10 and 3.13 pass). Both hooks on the branch's last 40 commits, each staged on its parent in a scratch clone (1 to 629 files): the same lines and exit code all 40 times, 0.1 to 0.9 s a run in Python and 0.2 to 1.8 s in node (34 of them fail the gate, on the repository's inert test samples and the registry store's SQL built from strings: what pre-commit's `exclude` is for). The npm CLI before and after `project.js`, on npm, eslint and pnpm as installed, `/opt/node-tools` (3,767 files with `--deps`) and this repository's sources and fixtures, with and without `--deps`: the same reports, terminal output and exit codes, in the same time.
- **CI leaves out one more of pratique's timed tests** (Oct 9; found in H-2's gates). `crypto::timing::a_coarse_clock_makes_the_harness_time_several_calls_together` (the library's B-98: on a coarse clock the timing harness times a batch of calls per sample) works out what a call costs from this machine's clock as it runs and expects the batch to span 10 to 45 ticks; here it spanned 7 (7 calls of 120 ns, a tick of 120 ns). It failed 2 times in 15 run alone on the 2-vCPU sandbox and 2 in 8 with the library's other tests (H-2's gates, g57, among them), and passed in the gates before. Nothing in it changed in `b2097695`, which added only ignored timing tests to the module. CI skips it as it skips `a_cancel_stops_waiting_and_running_requests_and_fails_new_ones`, until the library's next commit; the fix (a tolerance, a retry, or `#[ignore]` with the other statistical timing tests) is the library's, asked of it.
- **X-1: a robustness fuzzer for the JavaScript parser, and F-12** (Oct 9; the backlog's X-1, its js_parse target). `scripts/fuzz`'s `js-parse` runs `js_parse` (jsparse/parser.rs) on generated programs and mutants of the repository's own JavaScript (`js/src`, `js/test`): whatever the bytes, a tree or a well-formed `{"error": {line, reason}}`, in either dialect, the same twice, within the time limit — totality, determinism, linear time, as the lexer fuzzer has. A hand-written probe checked against V8 while it was written (`js_v8_compile.cjs`: V8 compiles each source as a script and as a module, nothing run) found F-12 (`docs/0.1.9-findings.md`): Annex B's HTML-like comments, which V8 reads in a script (Node's CommonJS: a `.cjs`, and a `.js` whose package.json does not say `"type": "module"`), were syntax errors, so a file that opened with `<!-- a banner` was read by the text followers only, without what only its tree answers (D-9's rewrites, a program carved out of another file, D-13's loads, the cross-file pass). Fixed in the parser's `skip()`: in JavaScript `<!--` is a line comment wherever a token may begin and `-->` one at a line's start, as V8 reads a script (V8 refuses a module that holds either; Bun 1.3 refuses `<!--` everywhere). TypeScript keeps `<!--` as tsc reads it, `<` `!` `--` (tsc 6.0.3 compiles `z = 5 <!--y, f()` to a call of `f`), and the supply-chain facts, which read a TypeScript file's text as JavaScript first, read the text as tsc does where the JavaScript reading took a `<!--` after a token for a comment (`Tree::html_after_code`, `jsparse::parse_with`), the comment's reading standing where tsc's does not parse; `-->` at a line's start is never code, and a comment in either dialect. The exact bytes only: `a < !b` and `a-->b` are untouched. Parse-parity with V8 is not yet the contract (the parser bounds nesting, and refuses an assignment or update to a call and `for (let in x)`, which V8 compiles: the backlog's JS-PARSE-STRICT), so V8 holds the parser to a curated list (`tests/architecture/test_jsparse_v8.py`), and `jsparse/tests.rs` (three tests) and `jsflow/supply.rs` (one) pin the semantics. The one jsparse_cases snippet that holds an HTML comment flips from an error to a tree: the only case that moved in `js_parse` (9,743 outputs) and `js_parse_spans` (84), by `scripts/snapshot.py diff` against the engine before. 41 fuzz targets now. Reviewed before it was applied: a first version read `<!--` as a comment in TypeScript too (a `.ts` file tsc runs could hide code after one from the tree, whose answer then stands in for the text followers'), described a file the parser refuses as read by none of the supply-chain tests, and seeded the target with no file of the repository (its root was one folder short; `test_fuzz_scripts.py` now checks it). Measured: the full gates (g59, on these commits) against the first version's (g58, itself identical to g56's): the benchmark (945), the popular set (1,205), Part F's sets (498 crates, Go's standard library, 4,104 Ubuntu units) and extra43 identical; the 11 recorded snapshot modules, Lazaret's cargo tests (436: the first version's 433 and three new), pratique's (1,532, its two skips), the WebAssembly check and build, the Python suite module by module (the WebAssembly parity test and the two guard modules past the 45 s box pass class by class) and in one process (5,668 tests), the npm package's tests (84 files) and the touched modules on 3.10 and 3.13 (121 tests). `js-parse` with the repository's seeds: 124,780 runs over three seeds, no finding. A parser fix, no rule change.
- **X-1: the Python parser's and the taint pass's robustness fuzzers** (Oct 9; X-1's last two js_parse-family targets). `scripts/fuzz`'s `py-parse` runs `py_parse` (pyparse/) and `taint` runs `taint_scan` (taint.rs, project mode's intra-file taint) on generated inputs and mutants of the repository's own sources: a tree (`{"_type": "Module"}`) or a well-formed `{"error": {line, reason}}` for `py-parse`, a well-formed list of the engine's positional issue rows (a `T-*` rule, a severity, a line) for `taint` in Python and in JavaScript (with JSX and without, as a `.ts` file is read), each the same answer twice, within the time limit — totality, determinism, linear time, as `js-parse` and the lexer fuzzer have. The parsers and the pass are held to CPython and to the recorded outputs elsewhere; these are robustness on bytes those do not reach. 43 fuzz targets now. Measured: `py-parse` 208,684 runs and `taint` 20,053 (it scans each input six times: Python, and JavaScript with JSX and without, twice for determinism) over three seeds with the repository's sources, no finding; `test_fuzz_scripts` passes on 3.11, 3.10 and 3.13 (g59). No engine, rule or snapshot change — test infrastructure only.
- **NET-1: pratique's production server commit, `f28b6d39`** (Oct 9; John pushed it as the library's phases 3 to 7 of its server, B-111 to B-114, and its scanning proxy, B-78). `scripts/sync_pratique.py ... --rev f28b6d39` takes it (614 files). Most of it is the server, behind the library's `server` feature, which no package of Lazaret's builds (`lazaret-net`'s tests use it): the HTTP/1.1 and HTTP/2 server, its runtime and ACME (`http::server`), the scanning proxy (`proxy`), and the tools and scripts of its conformance testing, which the sync leaves out (`tools/`). What changes for Lazaret's client, the network build: what tlsfuzzer found the library's TLS 1.3 doing short of RFC 8446 holds for the client too, which now refuses two things it took (a record whose plaintext, padding included, is over the limit; a record of another type inside a handshake message) and answers four it refused already with the alert the RFC names (an empty alert, a KeyUpdate whose request is neither 0 nor 1, a server Finished not the hash's length, a CertificateVerify in a scheme that does not go with the key); the HTTP/2 header decoder measures an indexed field before it copies it (a block of references to a large table entry, an "HPACK bomb", cost memory past the list's limit); and a proxy's login is percent-decoded, as urllib decodes it, so `nativenet._native_proxy`, Lazaret's reading of the native client's proxy setting (the credentials reviews' CR-12), decodes it the same way (`_pratique_decoded`): a proxy whose login holds `%40` goes over the native transport now, and one with `%+1`, which pratique's decoder reads as an escape (`u8::from_str_radix` takes a sign) and urllib does not, goes to urllib (`test_nativenet`; checked against the built library through a proxy that records what it is sent). New client options Lazaret does not use: `Client::follow_redirects`, `Client::resolve_host`. The pure part (`lazaret-verify`) gains `revocation::ocsp_response_is_good` and `TrustStore::certificates`, and nothing it did changed. Lazaret's asks of the library stand (`Url`'s `Debug`, `+443`, a backslash in the authority, the proxy's port and NO_PROXY, the two tests CI skips), with one more: `%+1` in a proxy's login. The library's `PROXY_CONFIGURATION.md` (how Lazaret should run its scanning proxy and point package managers at it, on each kind of network) is written to move into Lazaret's documentation; Lazaret does not run the proxy yet (the backlog's GUARD-PROXY). Measured: the full gates (g60) against X-1 v2's (g59): the benchmark (945), the popular set (1,205), Part F's sets (498 crates, Go's standard library, 4,104 Ubuntu units) and extra43 identical; the 11 recorded snapshot modules, Lazaret's cargo tests (436), pratique's (1,607, up from 1,532 with the server's own tests; its two skips), the WebAssembly check and build, the Python suite module by module (the WebAssembly parity test and the two guard modules past the 45 s box pass class by class) and in one process (5,668 tests), the npm package's tests (84 files), and the touched modules on 3.10 and 3.13 (146 tests: test_nativenet, test_tls_floor, test_guard_native_fetch, test_keepalive, test_sync_pratique); the network fuzz targets (go-sumdb-check, provenance-npm, credential-path, verify-answers, verify-credentials) found nothing.
- **F-13: `lazaret-sca --update-bundle` failed over the native transport** (Oct 9; found by the live checks, item 8, on John's Mac: `docs/0.1.9-findings.md`). The SCA's feeds have gone over the native transport since NET-1's second part, each with its own byte budget; an OSV export's was 4 GiB, and lazaret-net refuses a budget past its `MAX_BODY`, 2 GiB, before it connects, so every update failed where the native library is. The feed tests read local files only. Fixed: an OSV export's budget is the most the native transport carries (`nativenet.MAX_BODY`, held to lazaret-net's by a test); on Oct 9 the exports were well within it: npm's, the largest, was 208 MiB (230,260 records), PyPI's 34 MiB, Go's 12 and crates.io's 3.4.; a request whose budget is past it goes to urllib instead of failing (only a setting asks for one: `LAZARET_MAX_DOWNLOAD_BYTES`, `--max-download-bytes`); the guard's stream limit is the same constant; lazaret-net's comment on it, which said the Python side's largest budget was 500 MB, says what it is. `test_sca_feeds` fetches each feed's budget over the native transport from a local HTTPS server (the OSV one failed before the fix) and checks each is within `MAX_BODY`; `test_nativenet` holds the constant to lazaret-net's and a larger budget to urllib. Measured: the modules around the change on 3.11 (test_sca_feeds, test_nativenet, the guard's native fetch, keep-alive, the sources, the source scans, the TLS floor, the SCA's other modules, secret verification's transport and the pratique sync: 684 tests), the changed modules on 3.10 and 3.13 (137 tests each), the layering, portability and notices checks, and the whole Python suite in one process (5,673 tests; the npm package's parity tests skip there, its WebAssembly not built in this tree); the new SCA test fails on the code before the fix with the live checks' error. The engine, the rules and the native library are unchanged (a comment in lazaret-net), so the gates' sets were not run again.
- **FIXTURE-PIECES: no line of the repository holds a provider's credential format whole** (Oct 9; the backlog's FIXTURE-PIECES, after GitHub's push protection refused the 0.1.9 line for made-up AWS and Stripe keys in two test files, whose history was rewritten). The other tests and scripts that wrote made-up AWS key ids, AWS's example secret key or Slack tokens whole (52 in 17 files: the npm package's config-secrets, report-format and verify-secrets tests, the JS parity, verifylive, stored-redaction, artifact-hygiene, config-secrets, dashboard (parity, redaction, tables), perf-regex, secret-verification (`test_secretverify`, its HTTP transport, `test_verifyscan`) tests, `scripts/fuzz/fuzz_targets.py` and `scripts/verifylive/verifylive.py`) write one character of each as an escape (`\x41` for an `A`): every string keeps its value (each Python file's syntax tree is the same as before, but for two regular expressions in `test_secretverify`, where `re` reads the escape as the letter), and no line holds the format. All were made up and already public on `origin/next`; this is the tip, not history. `tests/architecture/test_fixture_credentials.py` keeps it so: it reads every text file of the repository (pratique's vendored sources aside) for nine formats (AWS key ids, Slack, GitHub, Stripe, npm, Anthropic, OpenAI and Google keys, AWS's example secret key), and holds itself live by catching each format written whole and passing it written with a character escaped. The fixtures' policy says so (`tests/fixtures/README.md`, STRUCTURE.md §6, TESTING.md). Measured: the 14 changed and new Python test modules on 3.11, 3.10 and 3.13 (398 tests on each), the npm package's three changed test files (39 tests), the two secret-verification fuzz targets (195,402 runs, no finding) and the new check over the whole repository (3 s); each changed Python file's syntax tree compared with the one before. Tests, scripts and docs only: no engine, rule, package or snapshot change.
- **S-TOKEN-PEM: a private-key header counts only with its key right after it** (Oct 9; the backlog's S-TOKEN-PEM, found by the live checks: two BLOCKERs in the bundles of redhat.vscode-yaml 1.25.0, from jose's `importPKCS8`, which checks a string by its header, `pkcs8.indexOf('-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----') !== 0`). The token rule's PEM alternative is the header alone, and `token_has_material` took any run of 40 or more of base64's characters on the header's line or the next two for its key: a minified line, megabytes long, always has one. `scanfile.rs`: `pem_has_material` reads the key right after the header, past what a string or a concatenation puts there (`past_pem_separators`: blanks, the escapes `\n` `\r` `\t` with any number of backslashes, backslashes that end the line, quotes and a string's prefix before one (`b"`, `rb'`, `u8"`, Rust's `r#"`: `string_prefix_end`), `#`, `+`, `,` and `.`), on its line or, where the line ends with them, at the start of the first of the next two lines that holds anything (a comment's `*` `/` aside); an encrypted key's `Proc-Type:` counts as its key; and `token_col` goes on past a header without its key to the line's other tokens, which the header hid, the finding taking that token's column, in one walk over the line (`token.rs`'s `TokenPattern::iter`: `spans`, lazily, each part's search kept until the matches pass it; searching again from each header read the rest of the line each time, over 45 seconds for a line of 60,000 headers). The dashboard's twin (`pemHasMaterial`, `tokenCol`), and config files' on both sides (`configsecrets.key_follows`, `keyFollows`, which keep the mix of cases and digits config files ask of key material; `key_material` and `keyMaterial`, read nowhere else, are gone), each header's key looked for in place on its line (the old reading searched a copy of the rest of the line for each header: over 45 seconds for that line, about 0.2 now, on both sides). `ENGINE_VERSION` and the pack's `rule_set` 2.65.0. Tests: `tests/scanner/test_private_key_material.py` (new: jose's check on a minified line with base64's runs after it, in both modes; the header as a constant, a path on the next line; the key in an escaped string, on the header's line, on the next line, in strings put together, in bytes put together (ecdsa's test keys' shape), in Rust's raw strings, in a list of lines, commented out, on a line continued, after PHP's `.`, encrypted on lines of its own and on one line; nothing past the next two lines; an AWS key after a header without its key; config files: a service account's JSON, a placeholder, a note, a .pem, an encrypted .pem; `key_follows` itself; six of its checks fail on the engine before), its twins in `js/test/scanner/scan.test.js` and `js/test/config-secrets.test.js` (two tests fail on the WebAssembly before), `test_review_dashboard_parity` (`pem_jose.js`, `pem_strings.js`, `pem_lines.py`: the page with the old logic differs from the CLI), the adversarial tree of `test_js_parity` (`cfg/pem-note.yaml`, `cfg/enc.pem`), `test_suppression_integrity.py`; a line of 60,000 headers and an AWS key in each reading, under ten seconds (about a second at most here). The recorded snapshots changed in S-TOKEN alone, and in S-ENTROPY with it (a line with an S-TOKEN gives none): scan_file and scan_rules in 65 of 4,259 cases (18 S-TOKENs more, each a token after a header without its key on the line; 48 fewer, each a line whose only tokens are headers with no key right after them, the base64 further on the line or on the next lines), scan_file_go_rs and scan_rules_go_rs in 42 of 1,552 (13 more, 31 fewer: the same two kinds, and one key in a Rust raw string at the start of the next line), scan_project in 25 of 3,005 (3 more, 23 fewer); recorded again. Measured: every file of the popular set's 1,205 releases and the benchmark's 945 that holds a private-key header (46 and 88 files), scanned by the engine in dependency mode before and after: in the benign releases, seven S-TOKENs more and none fewer, each an encrypted key in a library's tests (future's two `.passwd.pem`, pycryptodome's and pycryptodomex's encrypted DSA and RSA keys, jwcrypto's), and in 63 malicious ones 60 fewer, each a header in a credential stealer's code (`isPkcs1(t){return t.includes("-----BEGIN RSA PRIVATE KEY-----")}`, a regex that captures a key), none a key; the first build missed three of ecdsa 0.19.2's test keys (`b"-----BEGIN PRIVATE KEY-----\n"` and the key on the next line as `b"…"`: the `b` was read as the key's start), which a string's prefix now covers. Not read now, as the old rule read it: a key written by a command per line (`echo "-----BEGIN …"`, then `echo "MIIE…" >>` on the next), whose key is not at the next line's start. The installed npm trees with `--deps` (`/opt/node-tools`, `/opt/node22/lib`, `~/.npm-global/lib`: 1,301 findings, the same before and after; none holds jose). Gates (g62) green: the build, the Rust dependency check, pratique's copy verified, the tables (3.11 and 3.10), the CSP, 11/11 recorded snapshot modules, the workspace's 437 cargo tests and pratique's 1,607, the WebAssembly check and build, the npm package's 84 test files, the Python suite module by module (343, and four past the 45 s box under the gate's load, `test_scanpool` among them, whose 56 tests passed but whose process waited at exit on leaked semaphores, as it once did Oct 3; their 47 classes green) and in one process (5,693 tests), the touched modules on 3.10 and 3.13 (205 each), the fuzz targets (366,000 runs, no finding); against g60, the benchmark (945), the popular set (1,205), Part F's sets and extra43 identical (their records carry the supply-chain indicators, which S-TOKEN is not).
- **OVSX-LATEST: with no version, Open VSX's newest release, as the editors install it** (Oct 9; the backlog's OVSX-LATEST, found by the live checks: `lazaret-registry scan openvsx:redhat.vscode-yaml` scanned 1.25.2026100908, a nightly pre-release, where `vscode:` took the release). What the API calls latest is the newest version of either kind: read through a fetcher on Oct 9, redhat.vscode-yaml's document is that pre-release, with `versionAlias` `["latest", "pre-release"]`, and its releases (1.24.0, 1.23.0, …) come after its nightlies in the version lists, newest first by SemVer; rust-lang.rust-analyzer's newest release, 0.3.2053, comes after some 1,500 pre-release versions, 13,140 files in all, and each file of the query API's answer carries up to a hundred of the versions' URLs (`allVersions`, about 9 KB). `registry/ecosystems/openvsx.py`: when the latest is a pre-release and no version was asked for, `_newest_release` reads the query API in pages of `RELEASE_PAGE` (50) files, newest first, at most `MAX_RELEASE_SEARCH` (500), to the first page that holds a release (one the registry serves), and takes the newest release there by SemVer (`editorcompat.version_key`); that version's document is then read as a version asked for is. With none, the pre-release is scanned, and `registryInfo.preReleaseReason` says why ("the extension has no release", or none among the newest 500 files); `vsmarketplace.py` gives the first reason too, and `registry_lines` prints it ("…: a pre-release version, taken with no version asked for: …"). The guard (`candidates`, `editorcompat.choose`) is unchanged. README, DESIGN.md. Tests: `test_openvsx`'s `LatestReleaseTests` (the newest release by SemVer from the query's first page, a file the registry does not serve left out; the search going on to the first page that holds a release and stopping there; no release at all, and none among 500 files, each with its reason and its line; a release that is the latest, and a pre-release asked for, read with no query; three fail on the code before), `test_vsmarketplace` (the reason). Seen on the way: the history (SC-NEW-DEPENDENCY) and the guard read the query API in pages of 1,000 files, which for such an extension come to some 9 MB, past `MAX_DOCUMENT_BYTES` (5 MB): a new backlog item (OVSX-PAGE), to be checked against the live registry. Measured: S-TOKEN-PEM's gates (g62), which ran on this commit (above), `test_openvsx` and `test_vsmarketplace` among the modules run on 3.10 and 3.13.
- **JS-PARSE-STRICT: the parser reads what V8 compiles, nesting aside, and a registry scan lists what it refuses** (Oct 9; the backlog's JS-PARSE-STRICT, found reviewing F-12, R48-6: the parser refused an assignment or an update to a call, sloppy `for (let in x)` and nesting past its bound, and a file Node runs that it refuses keeps only the text followers, in silence). Measured first (g62's engine; `js_loads` on every `.js` `.cjs` `.mjs` `.jsx` `.ts` `.tsx` `.mts` `.cts` member but `.d.ts`, `$SP/jps/measure.py`): of the popular set's 23,645 files (249 M characters) and the benchmark's 13,845 (645 M), the parser refused 11, and V8 (`scripts/fuzz/js_v8_compile.cjs`) refused each of them too: six `.d.cts` type declarations of @jridgewell's (never run) and five broken files of malicious releases (two unterminated strings, a `#!` on line 2, a `...` out of place, a fixture named syntax-error). Probed against V8 the same day: it compiles an assignment, a compound assignment or an update to a call (`f() = 1`, `f() += 1`, `f()++`, `--f()`, `(f()) = 1`, `for (f() in x)`), in scripts and modules, strict or not, and throws a ReferenceError when the line runs, the call made first; it refuses a logical assignment to a call, a call inside a pattern, `new f()`, an optional chain, a tagged template, `import()`, `(await f())` and a sequence as a target; `let` before `in` and `instanceof` is a name in a script (`for (let in x)`, `let in x`). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/jsparse/expr.rs`: `assign_target` (`=`: a call, or a pattern as before) and `update_target` (`++`, `--` and the compound assignments: a call, or `simple_target`'s), a logical assignment keeping `simple_target`'s; `parser.rs`: `let_declares` (a statement's or a for head's `let` before `in` or `instanceof` is not a declaration), and a for-in or for-of head takes a call as its target. `jsflow/eval.rs`: an assignment to a call makes the call and stops there, as V8 throws before the value on the right is worked out; a loop's call target is made. The nesting bound was measured against the stacks the engine parses on (a build with `MAX_DEPTH` 4,096, each construct of `NESTINGS` given to `js_parse`, `js_loads`, `import_time_risk`, `scan_file`, `install_script_risk` and `js_flow`; `$SP/jps/probe`): natively, on the engine's own 8 MiB thread, every construct read to that bound; in WebAssembly on Node's main thread (V8's stack, about 984 KB) the calls stopped ("Maximum call stack size exceeded", which the npm package reports as the engine's error) from 992 nested tagged templates, the costliest construct, and every construct read to a depth of 976, 3.8 times 256. So `MAX_DEPTH` stays 256, and a deeper bound wants the npm package's engine on a thread with a larger stack (a Worker's `resourceLimits.stackSizeMb`: `docs/RUST_ENGINE.md` §12). V8 compiles deeper nesting (1,000 nested arrays, objects or blocks, 2,000 parentheses), the one kind of source it compiles that the parser now refuses. So the refusals are said: `jsflow/supply.rs`'s facts' cache keeps why there are none (`Unread`: the parser's refusal, its line and reason, or another reason: over 2 MB, past the pass's budget), and `refusal(text)` answers it; `api.rs`: `import_time_risk` asked with `refused` gives a third element, the refusal ([line, reason], or null), read from that cache, so the file is parsed once (600 of the npm files installed here, 7.8 M characters: the same time with it and without). `scanner/engine.py`: `import_time_risks(…, refused=True)`. `registry/repo.py`: `_import_risks` asks for it (the memo's key holds it); `_note_unparsed` keeps each member the import-time test or SC-USE-RISK's read and the parser refused (not a TypeScript declaration); `_unparsed_install_scripts` asks the parse alone of the JavaScript install scripts, a hook's targets and the scripts they start, whose test reads the tree too (`api.rs`'s `js_refusal`, `supply::parse_refusal`, which shares the model's parse, `supply_tree`); D-13 keeps a module `js_loads` could not read (it skipped it, "Node could not either"); `finish` gives them one SC-UNPARSED-CODE finding (INFO, `_unparsed_code_issue`: how many, the first three with their line and reason). README, DESIGN.md, `docs/0.1.9-findings.md` (F-12's "Left."), `docs/RUST_ENGINE.md` §12, CHANGELOG. `ENGINE_VERSION` and the pack's `rule_set` 2.66.0 (with the 2.65 line of the history, which S-TOKEN-PEM left out). Tests: `jsparse/tests.rs` (`a_call_as_a_target_and_let_as_a_name_as_v8_reads_them`: what is read, the call as the target, and what is refused, with the parser's reasons), `jsflow/supply.rs` (`what_v8_compiles_and_the_parser_refused_is_read`, `the_parse_alone_says_what_the_model_says_of_a_refusal`), `tests/architecture/test_jsparse_v8.py` (the new forms on V8's curated list; what V8 refuses, as a script and as a module, the parser refuses; nesting past the bound, the one place left), `tests/registry/test_unparsed_code.py` (new: a file Node runs nested past the bound, a broken one with its line and reason, an install script, one finding for five files, none where every file parses (a call as a target, `let` as a name, two declarations the parser refuses), the finding itself; four of its six fail on the engine before, as do the three new forms of `test_jsparse_v8`), `test_content_memo.py` (a content is asked once for each question: an install script's parse is a question of its own), `test_suppression_integrity.py`. The recorded js_parse snapshot changed in 4 of 9,743 cases, each now as V8 reads it: `for (let in x)` in a snippet parses, and three mutants' first error moves from an update of a call to the next token, which V8 refuses too (`a\n++;`, `(i).toLowerCase() ++ location`, `(String(…)) ++ p3`); recorded again. Gates (g63 on the first build; g64 on the commit as delivered, which adds the install scripts' parse, its last steps run again after the sandbox restarted), green: the build, the Rust dependency check, pratique's copy verified, the tables (3.11 and 3.10), the CSP, 11/11 recorded snapshot modules, the workspace's 440 cargo tests and pratique's 1,607, the WebAssembly check and build, the npm package's 84 test files, the Python suite module by module (345, and three past the 45 s box under the gate's load, `test_wasm_parity`, `test_guard_cargo` and `test_guard_go`, their 38 classes green) and in one process (5,701 tests), the touched modules on 3.10 and 3.13 (215 each), the fuzz targets with `js-parse` and `taint` (296,597 runs, no finding); against g62, the benchmark (945), the popular set (1,205), Part F's sets and extra43 identical (their records carry no INFO finding). Of the seven releases whose files the parser refused, four carry SC-UNPARSED-CODE now, two of them for an install script (`src/postinstall.js`, an unterminated string); no test reads the other three's (a test fixture, @jridgewell's type declarations).
- **The README's comparison, rerun for 0.1.9** (Oct 10; John: "Can you update the benchmarks for 1.9 release?", every row, the holdout now, the other tools' numbers kept from September). On `a768b48b`'s engine, with the September harness: the attribution run over the 945 (`run_attr.py`; litellm, whose scan is longer than a window, from g64's gate run: OK), the holdout (`run_holdout.py`, the 747 less the two examined Oct 2, aggregates only), the secrets corpus, OWASP BenchmarkPython (its expected results and the scorer; no test source read), SCA's eight projects against September's bundle and mirror, the guard end to end (`run_guard_npm.py`: each npm sample from a local registry) and the registry's dependency history (SC-NEW-DEPENDENCY from each sample's own manifest and the live registries). In-sample: 454 of 516 malicious releases SUSPICIOUS (88.0%; 0.1.8: 449), 444 on behaviour or technique (86.0%), 473 SUSPICIOUS or WARN (91.7%; 470), 471 with the dependency history (91.3%; 466: the 17 @mastra releases, whose versions npm has since unpublished and still dates). Popular: none SUSPICIOUS, 26 WARN (6.1%; 31): webpack, @vitest/mocker, cjs-module-lexer, google-adk and litellm to OK, each WARN for SC-B64 alone (N-4 and G-5). GuardDog's 365 high_risk: 364 SUSPICIOUS, cycalculator-ye51 WARN (a capture-the-flag task, as D-1 left it); either tool's strict verdict 455 of 516 (88.2%). Holdout: 639 of 745 SUSPICIOUS (85.8%; 636), 98.1% of them on behaviour or technique; the 497 sharing no code 399 (80.3%; 395), 432 SUSPICIOUS or WARN (86.9%), 389 on behaviour or technique (78.3%); the 248 siblings 240 (96.8%). Secrets 94 of 99 found and 6 of 72 decoys flagged, BenchmarkPython +0.215 (taint +0.165), SCA 1,278 of 1,278 and none extra: each as 0.1.8. The guard: 247 of 300 malicious npm releases blocked (82.3%; 241), the 246 SUSPICIOUS and @builderius/ai-builder, INCOMPLETE for the bytecode its install script runs (T-1); none of the 219 popular; median 1.34 s a sample, 95th percentile 4.03 s. README's comparison (a 0.1.9 column, 0.1.7's dropped) and the CHANGELOG's summary for the release.
- **`.gitignore` lists the engine an editable install builds** (Oct 10; John's `git status` on engine-0.1.9 listed `python/src/lazaret/_native/` as untracked). `build_editable` writes the library there for `lazaret.scanner._native`, and nothing listed the directory: `.gitignore` does now, and `scripts/make_bundle.py`'s walk (a tree without git) skips it as build output (`EDITABLE_NATIVE`); the wheel and the sdist already left it out. Tests: `test_build_backend` (`test_git_ignores_an_editable_installs_library`), `test_review_bundle` (the library in `JUNK`, walked and tracked) and `test_artifact_hygiene` (`test_is_junk_filters`, with `scanner/_native.py` still source); each failed before the change.
- **The READMEs, read for the release** (Oct 10; John: "Are our readme files properly updated?"). The root README's sections were current; its summary lines were not, and the two package pages were behind. PyPI's (`python/README.md`) still gave September's numbers (83%, 3 of 429 popular packages, 78% of 747): now 0.1.9's (88%, none, 86% of 745), with Go modules, crates and VS Code extensions among the registry's packages and the guard's provenance block. npm's (`js/README.md`) said the taint, SQL-sink and cross-file passes were the package's JavaScript and that a project's Python files were left out of the cross-file pass (`… Python files not analyzed`, gone since Oct 2): the engine runs them all, for Python files too, and only the config and data files' checks and the reports are JavaScript; `npx lazaret hook` is in its first example. The root README's commands and components name `lazaret hook` and the VS Code extensions, the npm package is the project scanner and the commit-time gate, the registry section no longer says the guard does not check provenance (it does, for a release published in the last 30 days), and the native engine runs a project's whole scan (Q-1). `docs/DESIGN.md` §1 the same.
- **An editable install replaces the engine as a new file** (Oct 10; found by the live checks: after `pip install -e ./python` rebuilt the engine for 0.1.9, `verifylive.py` was killed on John's Mac, "zsh: killed" and "Python quit unexpectedly"). `build_editable` wrote the library over the old one in place (`write_bytes`); macOS keeps the code signature it read for a file and, on Apple silicon, kills a process that loads a library whose bytes changed under it. `_replace_file` writes it beside the old one and renames it over (`os.replace`), so each install is a new file, and a failure leaves the old library and no temporary file. Tests: `test_build_backend` (a second editable install gives a new file, no temporary left; a failed rename keeps the old library), both failing before; and `pip install -e` twice in a fresh venv here: a new inode each time, `lazaret --version` answering 0.1.9 after each.
- **The release pull request's first checks: CodeQL's two polynomial regular expressions, and the npm hook tests on Windows** (Oct 10). CodeQL flagged `/[\\/]+$/` on the folder `lazaret hook` is given (`hook.js`, library input through `run()`'s `io.cwd`) and `/^"+|"+$/` on a Windows PATH entry (`lib/programs.js`, through `io.env`): each takes quadratic time on a long run of separators or quotes not at the end (12.8 s for 100,000). Both are loops now (`trimSeparators`, exported for its test, and `stripQuotes`, Python's `str.strip('"')` as `programs.py` has it), and a test reads such runs in under a second (12.8 s before). On Windows every repository test of `hook.test.js` failed at `git init`: git's user settings were `os.devNull`, `\\.\nul` there, which Git for Windows cannot read ("unable to access"); they are an empty file in a temporary folder now. The npm package's 81 test files and `test_js_parity_hook` pass here.

- **S-TOKEN-USR: Anthropic's `sk-ant-usr-` keys** (Oct 10; found by item 4 of the live checks: John's Anthropic key, `sk-ant-usr-…`, 106 characters, came back `unknown`, not in the table's format; John: "Yes let's put this in 0.1.9"). Public reports call them the Console's personal, identity-backed API keys, sent as `x-api-key` like `sk-ant-api03-`. S-TOKEN's Anthropic alternative takes `usr` as a kind beside three to five letters and two digits, `(?:[a-z]{3,5}[0-9]{2}|usr)`, in the rule pack's twelve copies, core's, the npm package's linear matcher (`redact.js`) and the dashboard's twin (its script hash anew); the verification table's Anthropic pattern is `sk-ant-(?:api03|usr)-[A-Za-z0-9_-]{20,200}`; rule set 2.67.0. Tests: a made-up key of the same shape (12, 76 and 5 characters between its dashes, built in pieces) in code and in config files, its near misses (39 characters, `usra`, `ust`), `identify` and the lengths in Python and Rust, the parity sets (`test_js_parity_tokens`'s edges and fragments, `test_wasm_parity_secrets`) and the fixture guard. Owed: `verifylive.py --only anthropic` with John's key, expected `live`.

- **The release pull request's checks, the second round** (Oct 13). CI failed `review-gyp.test.js`'s linear-time test: 80,000 command expansions in one string took 4,023 ms on the runner against a 4,000 ms budget. About 2 s here (1.9 s on Oct 1's engine: the hook commands' reading has grown since), twice that on a runner beside other test files; the quadratic form took minutes, so the budget is 15 s. CodeQL's list for the pull request: every new alert is in pratique's copy (hard-coded cryptographic values: RFC 9001's QUIC salt and Retry key, SHA-2's constants, zeroed buffers filled before use, test vectors, a benchmark; cleartext logging: assertions and `panic!`s in test modules, the test-only `fake_server`, the examples) or in Python tests (old TLS versions the TLS-floor tests offer on purpose, made-up credentials written to temporary files, test servers' replies), so they are dismissed as false positives or test code, and pratique's are its own repository's to scan. Of the older alerts, one was real: `js/src/scanner/scan.js`'s Python test (alert 2), quadratic on a run of line breaks (100,000: 9 s); its `^\s*` reads a line's own blanks now and `class \w+.*:` is `class \w.*:`, the same answers on 200,000 random texts, in the dashboard's twin too. The Postgres client's TLS alert is not (its minimum is TLS 1.2 or 1.3 by construction), nor its MD5 (the protocol's, refused without a verified certificate), nor `dashboard_csp.py`'s (it reads the repository's own page).
- **SC-INSTALL-HOOK follows a project's own install hooks** (Oct 14; decided for 0.1.9 the same day). A project scan judged the project's own hook by its command alone: the tensorlake worm's `"preinstall": "node lib/setup.mjs"`, in the project's own `typescript/package.json`, was MAJOR for being there. Now each install hook of every `package.json` that is not a dependency's (the install and prepare families) is followed as a dependency's is, to the files it runs and the scripts those start, and is CRITICAL when one fails the install-script test (`own`: the manifest's name) or is obfuscated (`is_obfuscated`, five `_0x` names, now shared with SC-AUTORUN); nothing in a tree the walk pruned is read (no dependency tree without `--deps`), and a file the walk did not read is read, not scanned. `core.project_hook_checks` (before `dependency_checks`, with its own stop: "what the project's install hooks run was not checked to the end") and `deps.js` `projectHookChecks`; `_started_dependency_scripts` takes the reader of a started script's text, and `_DependencyTree` the walk's pruned trees. Tests: `test_review_project_hooks` (8; five fail on the old code), `review-project-hooks.test.js` (one of its two fails on the old code), `test_js_parity_project_hooks` (in `ci.yml`'s parity job, as `test_review_release_workflow` requires); the `--deps` tests' first-party `postinstall` is CRITICAL now in both packages, and `test_js_parity`'s count of followed hooks is 11. Checked here: the Python suite module by module (350; `test_guard_cargo` and `test_guard_go` by class, being over 45 s whole here), the snapshot modules unchanged, the npm package's 85 test files, and Lazaret's scan of its own repository: 5,120 findings before and after, none new, the fixtures' hooks as before; six existing notes moved by a point or a few lines (`scan_project`, `decodeScript`, `autorunRisk`, `autorunScriptRisk`). Two modules fail the same on 615e6c1 here: `test_fuzz_scripts` (`archive-npm-diff`) and `test_npm_tar_readers`, both on a UNC or drive-relative root in the npm model (Python 3.13.16 here). Registry code and the engine are untouched. The check against the real commit (`github:tensorlakeai/tensorlake@e65ed94a`) is John's, on his Mac.
- **D-14: a dependency npm would fetch over plain http or from a raw IP address** (Oct 14; rule set 2.68.0; John chose it for 0.1.9). `core.dependency_url_risk` and `dependency_url_issues`, called from `scan_manifest`, and the npm package's `dependencyUrlRisk` and `dependencyUrlIssues` (`supplychain.js`): SC-DEP-URL (MAJOR) for a dependency given as `http:` or `git+http:`, or at a host that is an IP address (bracketed, or whose last label is a number, as the URL standard reads it). A package's root manifest is read for dependencies, optionalDependencies and peerDependencies (registry, guard, `--deps`), a project's own for devDependencies too; not a nested manifest, an extension's or an action's. `_script_key_lines` takes a section, so the finding is on its key's line. Measured first, here: the 801 npm releases of `scripts/popular/releases.jsonl`, their published manifests read from the registry (known-good packages, metadata only), 8,437 dependency specs: none. The five in-sample releases D-1 found are John's to check, on his Mac. Tests: `test_dep_url` (10; they fail on the old code), `dep-url.test.js` (3; fails on the old code), `test_js_parity_dep_url` (in `ci.yml`'s parity job). Checked here: the Python suite module by module (352; the cargo and go guard modules by class), the npm package's 86 test files, the pack (`--check`), and Lazaret's scan of its own repository: 5,120 findings before and after, none new (the tests write their `http:` stand-ins with one character escaped, as the fixtures' credentials are; `RegExp#exec`, which S-EXEC-JS took for child_process's, is `String#match` here); `scan_manifest`'s and the JavaScript locator's existing notes moved. `test_fuzz_scripts` and `test_npm_tar_readers` fail as on 615e6c1 (Python 3.13 here).
- **S-EXEC-JS leaves out a call on a regex** (Oct 14; rule set 2.69.0; offered by the session, accepted by John's "Do what you can do"). RegExp's own `exec` (`while ((m = re.exec(s)))`) was a CRITICAL "Shell exec" (D-14 met it in `DEP_URL_RE.exec(spec)`). The engine's `scanfile.rs` `exec_js_regex` (both packages) and the dashboard's `execJsRegex` leave a call out when its receiver is a regex literal the lexer read (its flags skipped: the two lexers differ on whether a literal's span takes them) or a name the file assigns a whole regex and nothing else (`_ASSIGN_NAME_RE`, any assignment operator; `_REGEX_CTOR_RE`, its parenthesis closed within `_REGEX_CTOR_REACH` steps on its line; the expression must end there, past a line break too). The first cut read a constructor to the end of its line, quadratic on a line of unclosed `RegExp(` (160 kB: over 6 seconds); bounded, 320 kB takes 0.2 seconds, and each line's comment spans are searched, not walked. A first version also asked whether the file names child_process or shelljs or requires a computed name, and dropped every member call in a file that did not: the snapshot sets showed it losing `cp.exec(…)` findings (12 outputs), and a file can be handed child_process by another (`require('./run').exec(cmd)`, `require('child_' + 'process')`), so it was withdrawn: where a receiver came from is not read, and `db.exec(sql + where)` counts as before. Not covered, on purpose: a name shadowed by a parameter or a destructuring of the same name elsewhere in the file. Tests: `test_exec_js` (9, one of them the time; the regex cases fail on the old engine), `exec-js.test.js` (5), three dashboard parity cases (the twins agree). The recorded snapshot sets are unchanged (13,056 outputs). Lazaret's scan of its own repository: 5,120 findings to 5,107, 13 S-EXEC-JS gone, none new; the 143 left are 122 test-file examples in strings and 21 regexes made by `pyRe(…)` or passed in, which a name does not show. No verdict reads S-EXEC-JS (a registry verdict counts SC- findings only), so the benchmark's verdicts are unchanged by construction; the rule set moves so that stored reports are scanned again.
- **X-CMD: a property that holds only a regex** (Oct 14; rule set 2.70.0; found by the session checking B-7 on vite 8.3.2, a known-good package, and taken up with John's "work on the stories"). A project scan of vite 8.3.2 reported CRITICAL X-CMD from the dev server's `req.headers["if-none-match"]` (`dist/node/chunks/node.js:20207`) to convert-source-map's `exports.commentRegex.exec(comment)` (line 17276), a getter in the CommonJS wrapper's `exports` that returns a regex literal. `jsflow/eval.rs`: `regexp_receiver` takes a property (`regexp_member`, `regexp_member_proof`, `define_property`, `regexp_descriptor`, `regexp_value`, `global_ident`), one answer per binding and name (`Mod::regexp_props`); `jsflow/mod.rs` keeps each member assignment's right side (`Mod::member_rhs`). `RegExp` and `Object` are read as globals by `bind_at`, not by a scope: the first cut looked one up with `scope_at`, which only scope-opening nodes have, and panicked; the panic ended the pass with a Q-FLOW-INCOMPLETE note and the test's "no flow" passed for it, so both twins' helpers now fail on a pass that did not finish. Tests: `test_review_flow_js_regexp` (two new sets, 21 cases; the seven proofs fail on the old engine; "a property holding a regex" moved from the sinks to the proofs), `review-flow-regexp.test.js` the same. vite's scan: 1,731 findings to 1,730, that X-CMD alone. The js_flow snapshot sets: one review case of 317 moved (`o.re.exec(p)` no longer the sink; the next line's `re?.exec(p)` is), re-recorded. Checked here: the Python suite module by module (353; the cargo and go guard modules by class), the engine's Rust tests (394), the npm package's 87 test files, the pack (`--check`), the other snapshot sets, and Lazaret's scan of its own repository: 5,107 findings to 5,106, one test string's S-EXEC-JS gone, none new. On the way: the S-EXEC-JS commit's test docstring had a line over 160 characters, edited after that commit's own scan (one Q-LONGLINE); rewrapped here.
- **The Python parser: an expression after a `with` item's target** (Oct 14; found by `test_pyparse_native_b` once GH-NEW-REPOS's files changed its seeded mutations of the repository's sources: `with … as ro{t:` read on into a dict whose next key had no `:`, which Python reports two lines down). `pyparse/parser.rs` (`with_item`: a target followed by a token that starts an expression is read as Python's error pass reads it, `adjacent_expression`, before the generic "invalid syntax"); `expr.rs` (`adjacent`, `adjacent_expression`: `pub(super)`); tests in `pyparse/tests.rs` (the dict's key, the missing comma in brackets, and the generic error outside them), checked against Python 3.13. No rule set change: the line reaches a report only as the taint pass's "syntax error at line N".
- **GH-NEW-REPOS: new public GitHub repositories, found and filtered** (Oct 14; John asked for it after X-CMD, from `specs/lazaret-github-new-repos-plan-2026-10-14.md`; John put it in 0.1.9 the same evening). `registry/ghdiscover.py` (new): the listing (`list_page`, `frontier`: doubling then halving on `since`), the run's `Budget` (`--max-calls`, `X-RateLimit-Remaining` at `RATE_FLOOR`, a rate-limit answer), `Filters` (`lookalike.github_lookalike` and `github_tables` on popular_names.json's new "github" section and the "go" targets on github.com; a copy with the original's description; `campaign_markers.json` (new), compared whole with case and separators folded, never downloaded; `--github-filters`), the content checks at `CHECK_HOURS`, the scan (`sources.checkout`, `core.scan_project`, `decide_verdict` with `HARDENING_RULES` below CRITICAL left out, a checkout not read whole truncated); `repo.py`: `--ecosystem github` (with `--resume`), `--max-calls`, `--github-filters`, `_discover_github`, `list`'s candidates, `parse_spec` and `scan`/`scan-all` for `github:`; the `github_candidates` table in SQLite, Postgres and `schema.sql`; `sources._http` and `Client.get` hand back the answer's headers (`reply_headers`). `scripts/fetch-github-targets.py` (new) and `update-popular-names.py --github`: the npm and PyPI targets' repositories, fetched here (known-good metadata) — of the 5,000 npm targets 4,919 name a GitHub repository (3,161 distinct; 4 could not be read), of the 5,000 PyPI targets 4,418 (3,659; 2), 6,813 repositories in all, with no descriptions until the most-starred list is fetched; GitHub's most-starred list, with the descriptions the copy test needs, is John's to fetch (`--stars`, a token; GitHub's API refuses this sandbox). Tests: `test_ghdiscover` (29 tests; a fake GitHub, the hostile repositories made of the existing inert fixtures). Not built: GH Archive for repositories made public, forks of popular repositories, re-scans after later pushes (phase 2), and the measuring day, John's. Checked here: the Python suite module by module (354 modules; the cargo and go guard modules by class), the engine's Rust tests, the npm package's 87 test files, `make_rust_tables.py --check`, and Lazaret's scan of its own repository: 5,106 findings to 5,106, none new; eight size notes moved on functions it grew (`repo.py`'s `cmd_discover`, `cmd_scan`, `main` and `parse_spec`; `update-popular-names.py`'s `main`).
- **Every benchmark set in one command, and `--jobs`** (Oct 14; John: the benchmark's long runs on his Mac rather than in a session, and "do build the one-command runner with --jobs"; with the rule of the same day that no session fetches or scans real malicious releases, the benchmark's and the holdout's runs are his). `scripts/bench.py run --jobs N`: N releases at once, each in a worker of the guard's pool (`scanpool.WorkerPool`: the engine's threads shared out among the scans running, a worker lost or hung an error row for its release alone, `Unavailable` scans in this process with a note), the worker running `record` by the module's name (`scripts/` appended to `sys.path`, so no script hides a module), the parent writing each line; a scan out of time beside the others is held back and scanned again alone after the rest (T-1's reading: a busy machine is not a verdict), and a finished run is rewritten in the manifest's order, so its file is the one a single job writes, byte for byte. `scripts/bench.py all SETS.json`: the sets file names each set's manifest (the holdout with `aggregate_only`), the runs' folder and the reports' folder; the engine library is checked first (`pack.info`'s rule set against the pack's, its build time against the newest file of `lazaret-engine` and `lazaret-ffi`: the editable install of Oct 10 that was not rebuilt; `--allow-stale-engine`, recorded in the summary), the run is labelled with the commit (and a digest of `git diff HEAD`), a label's folder keeps the library's digest and the deadline and refuses another, each set's run is compared with its last finished run (`history.json`; `--baseline LABEL`) and the comparison and `summary.txt` written to `REPORT/LABEL/`; the holdout's in counts only, and the progress lines and the summary are counts. Tests (`tests/build/test_bench_script.py`, 16, on the inert stand-ins): one job and two workers write the same bytes over npm, Go, crate and extension releases; a scan out of time is run again last and written once; a lost or hung worker, no workers, the caller's timeout with `--jobs`; `all` twice with a change between (the holdout's report, the console and the summary name none of its releases), `--only`, `--baseline`, a stopped run going on, another engine refused, the sets file's mistakes; the staleness reasons and the label in a scratch git repository. No engine, rule or registry code changed.
- **The benign sets fetched in parallel, over Lazaret's own client** (Oct 14; John, running `packaged.py ubuntu` on his Mac: "Getting the Ubuntu packages seems quite slow -- is our process threaded?", then "Are we using the rust pratique engine to download"). Both scripts fetched over urllib, a new connection for each file, one file at a time. `scripts/popular/fetching.py` (new): `Opener`, urllib's urlopen on the native client (`nativenet.open_stream`: connections kept alive, the caller's host rule on the URL and every redirect, a request marked `download` never asked for compressed, `max_bytes` its budget; a status other than 200 an HTTPError and no answer a URLError, as urllib raises them; urllib's opener where the native client is not used), and `each` (work N at a time in threads, the results in order). `popular.py`: `urlopen` is that Opener with HOSTS and `_Redirects`' urllib opener behind it; `fetch` and `pin` take `--jobs`; `_pace` is thread-safe (each thread takes the next free moment); a partial download is a file of its own (`mkstemp`; it was `.part-PID`, one per process). `packaged.py`: `ubuntu --jobs` and `--mirror` (https, a host, no login; the index and every `.deb` still checked against the pinned sha256), the Opener confined to the archive's or the mirror's host, `store`'s and `download`'s partial files their own. Measured here on 130 packages (`--only golang-github-a`, through this sandbox's proxy, two cores): 79.4 s over urllib one at a time, 26.5 s over the native client eight at a time (the downloads alone 61.9 s and 5.6 s), the manifests identical (119 Go modules, the same ids, digests and order). Tests: `test_popular_fetching` (7; the Opener against a local HTTPS server: a body read as urllib reads it, a download not compressed and a document compressed, a 404, a redirect off the rule, a body over its budget, urllib's opener when the native client is not used, twelve fetches four at a time; `each`), `test_popular_script` (fetch with eight jobs writes what one writes; six threads keep the pace; the default opener), `test_packaged_script` (four jobs write what one writes, no partial file left; a mirror and the ones refused; the default opener confined to the mirror's host). Also: `docs/TESTING.md`'s popular-set counts are John's run of Oct 14 (43 WARN of 1,703 on rule set 2.70.0; the 32 npm and PyPI WARNs it gave were before N-4's SC-B64 change took five to OK).
- **The benchmark's malicious releases and a holdout, rebuilt from DataDog's dataset** (Oct 15; John: "I need to be able to download the test samples to test locally", then "Can you create that short script?"). The sets lived in the September cloud environment, which is gone; the harness's `prepare_corpus.py` (`Claude outputs/lazaret-benchmark-harness-3.zip`) drew the benchmark, and the holdout's draw of Sept 30 was never packaged. `scripts/datadog.py` (new): the harness's draw unchanged (`pick_malicious`: equal on a made-up listing of 3,594 paths, and pinned in the test), its release rules (`release_root`, `release_files`, `release_artifact`, `build_from_raw`) on the zip read in memory, equal to the harness's `unzip -P` run on 30 inert samples, member names and bytes (the one difference: a damaged `.zip` at a release's top, on which the harness stops), and names read as unzip reads them (the UTF-8 flag, Info-ZIP's Unicode Path field, else the stored bytes). The zips come from git at the commit (`ls-tree`, `cat-file --batch`), so the clone needs no checkout; a blobless clone fetches the samples drawn in one request (their ids to `fetch --stdin`, as git's lazy fetch does) rather than one at a time. The holdout is a new draw of Sept 30's shape (seed 20261015: 300, 300 and 150 among the packages the benchmark does not hold); its first run is its baseline. `test_datadog_script.py`: the draw, each layout, the names, a run and its resumption, the refusals and a blobless clone (10 tests, Python 3.10 to 3.13). No session fetched or opened a real sample: the runs are John's. A check of the rebuild: at `a768b48b`'s engine the benchmark should come out at Oct 10's 454 of 516 SUSPICIOUS, or within a few (a faster machine finishes scans the sandbox ran out of time on). Then John, whose clone was a `--depth 1` one at the dataset's newest commit: "I wouldn't mind keeping the latest commit and restarting our measurements from there". `--commit` draws the sets at another commit with the same seeds and shapes; their holdout also leaves out the packages of the audit's benchmark (drawn at `b302ad0b`: the releases the detectors were written from), recorded in its `draw.json`, so a clone without that commit is refused with the command that adds the history without the old samples (`git fetch --unshallow --filter=blob:none origin`, run in the test on a shallow clone; it makes the clone a partial one, and the sets are then the full clone's). John's run: the dataset at `0cf258a43d8baf58aeb01a7e67dbdb63206dc554` (Oct 15), 517 releases drawn for the benchmark (the compromised PyPI releases are 17 there, 16 at `b302ad0b`). That commit is the script's default since, so the measurements' sets are drawn again without naming it; `--commit b302ad0b` gives the audit's.
- **ACT-BINARY: a binary GitHub's own actions ship is MINOR in the Actions check** (Oct 14; found in John's live run of Oct 10, `Claude outputs/live-checks/actions.txt`: `actions/setup-node`'s `externals/7zr.exe`, 7-Zip, which the action extracts Node with on Windows, was SC-BINARY MAJOR, once in `ci.yml` and twice in `release.yml`, and the command exited 1 on Lazaret's own workflows; every other action there was OK, and no SC-ACTION-* finding). `registry/actions.py`: `rule` gives a code finding of `FIRST_PARTY_SMALL` (SC-BINARY) at MAJOR in an action of `ghworkflow.FIRST_PARTY` (`actions/`, `github/`) as MINOR, as a version tag of theirs already was (SC-ACTION-NESTED-UNPINNED); a strong finding of their code is not lowered, and the same binary in another's action stays MAJOR. `main` exits 1 for a finding of `COUNTED` (MAJOR, CRITICAL, BLOCKER) and lists a MINOR one only (an official Docker image at a version tag and a GitHub-owned action at a version tag were MINOR already and made it exit 1). The same action used on two lines stays a finding on each line. Tests (`test_actions`, two new): the binary MINOR in `actions/` and `github/` and MAJOR in `o/act` and `actions-x/setup`, a decoded script run in `actions/setup-x` still CRITICAL; the exit 0 for a MINOR finding alone and 1 for the MAJOR one. Owed (John, with a token): the live run again, which should exit 0 on Lazaret's workflows.
- **BR-4: an archive path through `..` is CRITICAL** (Oct 14; John: "Let's treat BR-4 path finding as Critical", the decision BR-4's part 2 left him, Oct 9; rule set 2.71.0). `repo._archive_issue`: the kinds in `CRITICAL_ARCHIVE_KINDS` (`dotdot`: an sdist's or a wheel's member through `..` that pip resolves inside the folder) are CRITICAL, the others MAJOR as before; its `why` says so. A release with one is SUSPICIOUS, which the guard blocks; it was WARN unless the file's own tests found something. BR-4's part 2 measured the shape first: no release of the benchmark, the popular set, Part F's sets or extra43 holds one, so their verdicts cannot move. Tests (`test_pip_archive_paths`): a harmless file through `..`, in an sdist and in a wheel, is SUSPICIOUS with the CRITICAL finding, the same files without it OK; a `..` that leaves the folder stays MAJOR, and so do the other shapes. Owed (John): `bench.py all` on his Mac, all sets identical but for the rule set.
- **BR-4: npm 10's node-tar beside npm 11's** (Oct 15; F-14, found when `test_npm_tar_readers`' two root cases failed on this sandbox's Node 22; rule set 2.72.0). npm 10 (10.9.4, Node 22's) bundles node-tar 6.2.1, not the 7.5 `registry/npmtar.py` modelled: its parser reads as 7.5's, but its `Unpack` refuses a `..` part by `/` alone (on Linux and macOS `package/..\x.js` is a file named `..\x.js`, which main can require: the scan never read it, and the release was WARN) and takes a path's roots off before the first part is dropped (`C:x/index.js` is x/index.js, `package/C:index.js` a file of that name, `package//srv/x.js` none). `npmtar.written_path_npm10` is its placing; `disagreements` holds each entry to both (`WRITERS`), so one either npm writes elsewhere than the scan read it, or the scan did not extract, makes the archive INCOMPLETE. The fuzz target asks `npm_extract.cjs` for node-tar's version and predicts with that one's model; seeds 0 to 6 against node-tar 6.2.1 and 7.5.22 (`LAZARET_NPM_DIR`) find nothing. The popular set's 801 npm tarballs: none corrupt. Tests: `test_npm_tar_readers` (`Npm10Tests`, 4; the two root cases name what each npm writes; the module passes with node-tar 6.2.1 and with 7.5.22 as the oracle), `test_fuzz_scripts` (the npm model patched for both npms; the model is the bundled node-tar's). Owed (John): `bench.py all` on his Mac.
- **SC-NEW-INDICATOR: a release compared with its established predecessor** (Oct 15; John, Oct 14: "If a scan is risky looking and the prior scan was not should this inject a new signal?", then "if the latest release has findings then we review the prior release vs it"; rule set 2.73.0). Measured first on the popular set, here (benign: its 1,703 releases benched in the sandbox, 42 WARN as John's run of Oct 14 had 43): with each WARN release's established predecessor scanned, 41 had a finding of every weaker indicator's rule in it, and pnpm 12.8.1's postinstall (`node install.js`, against 11.27.1) was new; only binaries changed otherwise (ring's and winapi's new `.o` and `.a` files, SC-BINARY). `registry/prior.py` (new): `release_issues` (called by `scan_package` through `_release_findings`, which `scan_package`'s three lines of SC-NEW-DEPENDENCY and provenance became; `_release_fields` its result fields): `candidates` (the weaker indicators asked about, none when one is strong, `NOT_COMPARED` aside: SC-BINARY, SC-OPAQUE-BLOB, SC-NESTED-ARCHIVE), `established` (the newest release published `GAP`, 7 days, or more before, a pre-release only for a pre-release), the history by ecosystem (npm's packument, PyPI's Simple API with upload times (PEP 700), crates.io's index; `MAX_HISTORY_BYTES` 16 MB), `counterparts` (the predecessor's file of the same name with the version changed, else the first of the kind; `MAX_FILES` 3), each fetched, checked against the registry's digest and scanned with the run's memo; a new rule is SC-NEW-INDICATOR, CRITICAL, at the release's first finding of it; the result's `priorRelease` (the predecessor, how long before, its files, the new rules; or `notCompared` and why). `LAZARET_NO_PRIOR_RELEASE=1` turns it off (`tests/__init__.py` does, for the suite), as `LAZARET_NO_DEPENDENCY_HISTORY=1` does. `scripts/bench.py prior RUN MANIFEST OUT [--aggregate-only]`: a run's WARN releases scanned again and compared as a registry scan compares them (the predecessor fetched), by category; on the popular set: 33 asked nothing, 7 compared with nothing new, pnpm the one SC-NEW-INDICATOR, vite's packument (39 MB) over the budget. Live here: pnpm 12.8.1 SUSPICIOUS, coverage 7.16.2 compared with 7.16.1 (WARN), vite not compared. Tests: `test_prior_release` (13: npm, the hook new against a predecessor 30 days back with one of two days back beside it, the same hook in the predecessor, no established predecessor, a pre-release passed over, a digest that is not the registry's, a file gone, the packument over the budget, off, a strong release asking nothing; PyPI, the wheel of the same tags compared; crates, by the index, a yanked predecessor passed over; the parts), `test_bench_script` (`prior`). Owed (John): `bench.py all`, then `bench.py prior` on the benchmark's run (the malicious releases' predecessors are fetched from the registries; the holdout's with `--aggregate-only`), which says how many of its WARN releases the finding catches; the guard's part: below.
- **SC-NEW-INDICATOR in the guard** (Oct 15; the second part of the above, John's "then the guard"; rule set 2.73.0 as above, delivered with it). `registry/prior.py`: `compare(eco, name, version, asked, per, fetch_json, scan_file)`, the comparison over the caller's fetch and scan (`release_issues` is it over the registry's), `rules_found`. `registry/guard.py`: `_scan_one`'s answer keeps its findings' rules (`rules_of`: the first finding of each, in short); `prior_due` (a WARN with its rules, from the public registry, in `PROVENANCE_DAYS`, not compared yet), `with_prior` (`prior.compare` over the guard's fetcher, `_prior_scan`: the predecessor's file fetched under the byte gate, checked against the registry's digest, its verdict cached under its own key; a new rule makes the verdict SUSPICIOUS, its line first; `Check.prior`, `priorRelease` in --json), run in `check_npm_package`, `check_file` and the pip index after the provenance check; `VerdictCache.put` keeps the rules and the comparison once made (`_prior_kept`: one the registry did not answer is made again, from the cached rules, with no new scan of the release). Tests: `test_guard_prior` (6: the hook new against the release 30 days back, blocked, then known from the cache with nothing fetched; the hook in the predecessor; out of the window, a private registry's (nothing asked of npm's), off; a registry that did not answer, asked again; PyPI's wheel by the lockfile, both ways).
