Across modules the tree follows no such send yet, nor a plain call's (D-7's). Tests: `test_param_callbacks` (7: the arrow, a named function at another position, the caller's own value handed on, two hops; a constant given, a callback that sends nothing, the data given another parameter). Owed (John): `bench.py all` (the malicious sets: what it finds).
- **P-17, part 1: the tree's cost on a bundle** (Oct 15; the backlog's P-17, from D-8's measurement of Oct 10; no rule set change: the answers are the same). Profiled with callgrind on notebook 7.6.3's `notebook_core` bundle (2.5 M characters) and timed on the popular set's fourteen JavaScript files over 2,000,000 characters (benign: monaco 0.57.0's, plotly 7.1.0's, notebook's, jsdom 30.1.1's), with the limit raised to 16,000,000 in a scratch copy of the engine only. `jsflow/driver.rs`: `give_up` (the supply-chain model, whose answer is the text follower's once a reading is incomplete, stops at the first function cut, the cap met or the budget spent, and skips the reporting pass); `jsflow/supply.rs`: `eq_dotted` (`receives` and `dotted2` built a dotted name with `format!` for each client and call, for every call read: 8% of the bundle's instructions), `sc_names` made once per callee (`Program::sc_names_memo`); `jsflow/mod.rs`: `IdHasher`/`IdMap` (FxHash's multiply for the maps keyed by the pass's numbers). Measured (`examples`' timing of `local_data_sent_tree` and `local_data_sent_at`, the optimized build): 110.3 s on the tree before, 25.8 s after, the text follower about 6 s; monaco's TypeScript workers, unread either way (the iteration cap), 12.0 to 22.2 s before and 1.5 to 3.0 s after; the nine files read to the end 18.9 s and 15.9 s (callgrind on notebook's: 7.20 G instructions to 5.75 G before the hasher and the stop). Checked: the recorded outputs (`test_snapshot_hooks`, `_js_flow`, `_scanfile`, `_project`, `_small`, `_signs`), the engine's 395 tests, the popular set's 1,703 releases (no verdict moved; four that a concurrent build made time out read the same alone). Not met: P-17's goal, the trees within the text followers' time: the parse alone is half the text follower, and what is left of the evaluation is spread over its allocations. D-8 keeps the 2,000,000-character limit.
- **D-20: two false positives the tree gave on benign bundles** (Oct 15; the backlog's D-20, from D-8's measurement of Oct 10; rule set 2.75.0). Both files are over 2,000,000 characters, so the text followers answer for them today; read with the limit raised in a scratch copy of the engine (`examples`' `local_data_sent_tree`, `received_code_tree`, `import_time_risk`, `install_script_risk`). (1) notebook 7.6.3's `notebook/static/notebook_core.1038f6e3d817cc41.js`, line 508 "runs code it receives over the network": JupyterLab's translation package (`@jupyterlab/translation`'s `Gettext`, from gettext.js) fetches `api/translations` from its server and makes the plural forms a function, `getPluralFunc(e)`: `if (!RegExp("^\\s*nplurals\\s*=\\s*\\d+\\s*;\\s*plural\\s*=[\\s\\-?|&=!<>+*/%:;n0-9_()]+$").test(e)) throw …; return Function("n", "let plural, nplurals; " + e + …)`. What passes is arithmetic on `n`. `jsflow/supply.rs`: `inert_pattern` (a pattern from `^` to `$` of single characters, `\s`, `\d`, escaped punctuation and classes, letting through no quote, backquote, backslash, `.`, bracket or brace and at most one letter in a class or a repeat; no group, alternation, negated class, `\w`, `\b`, escape by number or code, and no `i`, `m` or `v` flag), `sc_checked` (after `if (!re.test(x)) throw|return` with no `else`, x is clean for CODE; `sc_failed_test`, `sc_regexp_source`: a literal, `RegExp(…)` of constants, a name given one once), `sc_sink` skips a value clean for CODE at a RUN_CODE sink. `jsflow/eval.rs`: the if statement calls `sc_checked` in supply mode; `regexp_receiver_with` (whether an eval or a with counts). `jsflow/mod.rs`: `Func::eval_with` (a direct eval or a with by function): the bundle has two direct evals (`evalInContext`, `eval("require")`), which made the module's `eval_with` refuse any `RegExp(…)`; only one around the test can make `RegExp` another name for it. (2) inspect-ai's `_view/dist/assets/index.js` (0.3.265, 5,958,953 characters; the measured release's view had both reasons, 0.3.280's has the first), in the install-script test: "reads files outside the package and sends them over the network (/app-config)" and "contacts an address typical of data exfiltration (.onion)". `_LD_FS_ROOT_RE` ended its system folders at `\b`, which holds before `-`: the route `/app-config` (`get_app_config`: `requestApi.fetchString("GET", "/app-config")`) was the folder `/app`, a path outside the package; the helper's `fetchFn(url)`, `customFetch ?? fetch`, a parameter's, returns what it was given, so every response held that path; a zip reader's `readFile(name)` (the script's own reader, by its name) given one was a file read, and react-router's `new Request(url, init)` its send. The `.onion` is linkify-it's doc comment (`.tlds('onion', true)`), which `label_sends` names once a send is found. The pack: `_LD_FS_ROOT_RE`, `_LD_ABSOLUTE_IN_RE` and `_LD_FS_ROOT_SRC` end a folder at a separator, a quote, `{` or `$` (`` (?=[/\\"'`{$]) ``), for the trees and the text followers. Measured: the three bundles (notebook's, inspect-ai 0.3.265's and 0.3.280's) answer nothing on the tree; the popular set's 1,703 releases moved no verdict and no finding (against D-19's run; monaco-editor 0.57.0 and google-cloud-aiplatform 2.3.0 time out under the harness in every run here, and read alone INCOMPLETE (the scan's budget) and WARN (SC-PTH-EXEC)). Not changed: the helper's fetch, a parameter's `customFetch ?? fetch`, still returns the URL's kinds, so a route under a system folder (`/data/…`) would be a path again. Tests: the engine's `what_inert_pattern_reads`, `a_text_that_passed_a_test_no_code_passes_runs_nothing_it_received`, `a_system_folder_is_the_folder_not_a_name_that_begins_with_it`; `test_bundle_false_positives` (6: JupyterLab's test, gettext.js's, one that lets letters through, none; a file under `/app/`, three names that begin with a folder). Owed (John): `bench.py all`.
- **D-11: JS-Confuser's string concealing in the decoded view** (Oct 15; the backlog's D-11, from panel-keylogger-sim 1.0.0; rule set 2.76.0). JS-Confuser 2 (2.0.0, December 2024; 2.1.3 now; MIT) replaces each string of three characters or more with `F(start, length)`, a slice of one long string (decoys around each piece) decoded with base91 over an alphabet each decoder shuffles (its `transforms/string/stringConcealing.js` and `encoding.js`, read). New `jcstr.rs` (`jc_read`), on the lexer's tokens: decoders (a function declaration holding one literal of the 91 characters, each once, and calling `indexOf`), retrievers (`function F(a,b){return D(X["slice"](a,a+b))}`, `.slice`, `b+a`), the long string (the one string literal its name is assigned: the renaming reuses names for other values), a call's arguments (numbers, decimal or hexadecimal, or items of a list of literals assigned once: the low preset's `C[0x11]`), each call written as its decoded slice where that is UTF-8 (`strarr::sa_quote`, now crate-public). `signs.rs`' `decoded_view_of` asks it after the string arrays, for any text but Python's; it names no string-array line (no technique reason). Fixtures (`tests/fixtures/js_confuser`, its README): an inert exfiltration (the host name and the environment to `collect.invalid`) and a greeting, written once by JS-Confuser 2.1.3 run here (string concealing alone, and the low preset). Measured: both obfuscated exfiltrations were OK (with the retriever not read) and are SUSPICIOUS, SC-IMPORT-RISK CRITICAL "(in strings it decodes as it runs)"; the greeting is OK; the popular set's 1,703 releases moved no verdict and no finding. Not read: the medium and high presets (the whole program a string `Function` runs, with other encodings), JS-Confuser 1.x's string array with a cached accessor. Tests: the engine's `jcstr` tests (2: what is read, what is not), `test_js_confuser` (6); the snapshots that read the fixtures (`scan_file`, `scan_rules`, `file_context`, `scan_project`) gain their five files' outputs, and without those files the recorded outputs hold. Owed (John): the benchmark's run (panel-keylogger-sim's strings decode, if it is JS-Confuser 2's string concealing).
- **D-11, part 2: JS-Confuser's string concealing is a sign of its own** (Oct 15; John, on D-11: "seems like we would want that to be Critical?"; rule set 2.78.0). As a string array is: the decoded view's reading carries the line of the file's first decoder (`signs::Reading` is now its text, a string array's line and that line; `concealed_strings_line`), and `install_script_risk` and `import_time_risk_with` give `_JC_TECHNIQUE_REASON`, "hides its strings in one long string it decodes as it runs (JS-Confuser's string concealing)", which `_STRONG_IMPORT_REASONS` holds (the pack's and core's): CRITICAL at install, a strong reason at import. Measured: the two obfuscated exfiltrations are CRITICAL with the technique among their reasons; the obfuscated greeting, OK before, is SUSPICIOUS at import (SC-IMPORT-RISK CRITICAL, the technique alone) and at install; a retriever not read stays as before. The popular set's 1,703 releases moved no verdict and no finding. Tests: `test_js_confuser` (7: the greeting's verdict, the reason at install).
- **EG-6: a bare require into the package's own node_modules** (Oct 15; the backlog's EG-6, from the second review of the extension model; rule set 2.79.0). vsce packs an extension's dependencies into its `.vsix` and Node resolves `require('dep')` to `node_modules/dep` in the requiring file's folder or the nearest above it, so what `main` loads by a bare name runs when the editor activates the extension; the walk followed relative requires alone, and such code had the use-time test only. `registry/repo.py`: `_JS_BARE_DEP_RE` (a bare specifier in a require, an import or an export-from), `_NODE_PROVIDED` (Node 22's built-in modules and `vscode`), `_ArtifactScan._js_deps` (the relative and the bare deps of a member, for `_reachable` and `_reach_of`), `_has_node_modules` (asked once: an archive without one reads nothing more), `_resolve_bare` (Node's walk up, a folder named node_modules passed over, `_resolve` at each; memoized by folder and specifier). An npm package's bundleDependencies, packed in its tarball's node_modules, are followed the same way. Measured: the popular set's 1,703 releases moved no verdict and no finding (two the harness timed out on under this sandbox's load, rerun alone, as before). Owed (John): the Open VSX benign set (`popular.py fetch --only openvsx --manifest DIR/vsix.jsonl`, then `bench.py run`; open-vsx.org is not reachable from a session): how many extensions gain an import-time finding in a bundled package. Not done: TypeScript loads a server plugin by name from every installed extension's node_modules, so one extension's plugin name can load another's package. Tests: `test_bundled_modules` (5: a bundled package the entry requires, the nearest folder and a subpath, a built-in, `vscode` and a package not held; an npm bundle dependency; no node_modules folder, nothing asked).
- **Q-2 and Q-3: BenchmarkPython's categories without a rule, and two pattern rules that flagged its safe code** (Oct 15; the backlog's Q-2 and Q-3; rule set 2.80.0). Scored with the audits' scorer (`score_sast.py` from `lazaret-benchmark-harness-3.zip`) on BenchmarkPython 0.1 at `f129148`, cloned here and scanned (`python3 -m lazaret bp --json …`); its test sources were not read (the project's licence rule: it is GPL). Its expected-results file gives each case's category and whether it is real; what the cases do was learned only from findings: probe rules (an id and a line, CWE-less, installed at run time with `pack.install`) correlated with the categories and with what the pass flagged. Baseline (0.1.9 before this): +0.215 (true positive rate 0.514, false positive rate 0.299). **Found and fixed in the taint pass** (Python): (1) `_PY_LIT_RE` read no escapes, so `'…=\'' + name + '\']'` hid `name` (xpathi 15 → 27 of 51 true positives); (2) a `for` statement bound nothing (`for name in request.args.keys():` then `param = name`: 28 real cases across the categories; `for_targets`, a target list of names, in parentheses or not, its iterable up to the header's colon); (3) `set()` was no write (configparser's `conf.set('s', 'keyB', q)` then `conf.get('s', 'keyB')`: 28 real cases, and 48 safe ones that read `keyA`): `_CONTAINER_WRITE_RE` takes `set`, `keyed_set` keys it by its literal arguments but the last (NUL-joined), and `keyed_reads` blanks a `get()` of literal keys whose entry holds no untrusted data (a dict's `get('k')` as `d['k']`). **New sinks** (`TAINT_SINKS['py']`, a seventh field naming the arguments read: `ArgMode::named`, a new `Rest`): T-TRUST (the session's keys and values: `session[k] = v` read whole, `update`/`setdefault`/`__setitem__`), T-LDAP (`search`, `search_s`, `search_st`, `search_ext`, `search_ext_s`, `paged_search` on a receiver, not `re.`/`regex.`; only in a file importing `ldap3` or `ldap`: `_SINK_IMPORTS`; partial sanitizers `escape_filter_chars`, `escape_dn_chars`, `escape_rdn`, `filter_format`), T-XPATH (lxml's `.xpath(`/`XPath(`, the first argument: its variables are keywords; elementpath's `select`/`iter_select`/`Selector`, the positional ones; partial sanitizer: the value's quotes replaced). **New rules**: S-WEAKRAND (pattern, HOTSPOT MINOR CWE-330, `random.<generator>(` on a line without S-RANDOM's words; S-RANDOM's Python names widened to the same list, so one of the two answers), S-COOKIE (project pass `cookie_flags`, after the SQL sinks: `set_cookie` whose `secure` is missing, by keyword or as the seventh positional argument, or False, 0, None; a `*`/`**` argument or an expression leaves it alone; read on `taint::statement_at`, the pass's statement joiner, now shared). **Q-3**: S-XML (XXE) is the external-entity features turned on and lxml's `resolve_entities=True`, VULN MAJOR, not the stdlib import (xml.sax and xml.dom resolve none since 3.7.1; Expat 2.4.1 refuses entity bombs): the 8 real XXE cases turn `feature_external_ges` on, as 7 of the 20 safe ones do. S-EVAL-PY is unchanged: in 22 of the 33 safe code-injection cases the probes found a check of the value's first and last characters (`if not X.startswith('<punctuation>') or …`, `endswith`), which is not a sanitizer for `eval` (a quoted string can close and reopen), and the taint pass flags 26 of the 33 safe and 18 of the 20 real; letting S-EVAL-PY yield to it would drop 7 false positives and 2 true ones. Result: **+0.541** (0.872 / 0.331; true positives 161 → 368 of 452, false positives 110 → 154 of 778): securecookie 0 → +1.00, weakrand 0 → +1.00, ldapi 0 → +0.71, xxe 0 → +0.65, xss +0.46 → +0.62, pathtraver +0.35 → +0.48, xpathi 0 → +0.41, trustbound 0 → +0.35, redirect +0.17 → +0.33; cmdi −0.15 (S-SHELL-TRUE on its 7 safe cases), codeinj 0.00, deserialization +0.67, hash +0.52, sqli +1.00 unchanged. Still missed: 28 real cases read the parameter with a call on another object given a string (`param = x.y('…')`, as 79 safe ones do), which only following the call into its helper can tell apart, the cross-file engine's work; redirect's 14 false positives check the parsed URL's host against a list (`urlparse(x).netloc in …`), which the allowlist guard does not read. The page's twins (`forTargets`, `keyedSet`, `keyedReads`' `get()`, `callArgs`, `taintStatement`, `cookieScan`, the rows' modes, `SINK_IMPORTS`). Tests: `test_taint_owasp` (9; in the dashboard parity test too), the engine's `taint_tests` (3: what a `for` binds, keyed `set`/`get`, a row's modes); the recorded snapshots hold (no corpus file has these shapes). Dependency scans read none of this: the popular set's 1,703 releases moved no verdict and no finding (one the harness timed out on, rerun alone, as before). Self-scan (against the same tree before): the new findings are in the new test module's own strings (S-COOKIE 6, T-TRUST 4, S-XML 3 …) and, from the `for` targets, T-PATH on five loops over a command line's or an environment's values (`for path in files:` in `registry/actions.py` and `scripts/dashboard_csp.py`, the fake cargo's plan), as the pass reports `sys.argv` elsewhere; S-XML's 28 import findings in the safexml tests are gone (S-XML 28 → 5), one S-WEAKRAND (a test generator's `random.random()`); Q-FN-CX and Q-FN-LONG unchanged.
- **D-3c: what a method keeps in a member of `this`, per receiver** (Oct 15; the backlog's D-3c, from D-3b; rule set 2.81.0). D-3b put nothing a method holds of its own parameters into a member of `this` (`sc_put_into`'s `within(none)`), since the class-wide member then held what every caller recorded (monaco-editor's LoaderEventRecorder: its events reached the loader's check that eval runs). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/jsflow/`: a method's summary keeps, by parameter, the members of `this` it puts it into as into a container (`Func::param_kept`, `sc_keeps`, from `sc_put_into`; through `this.m(x)` in another method, the caller's parameter too) and the members it returns as written (`Func::ret_kept`, `sc_returns_member`); a call on a name puts each kept argument into that name and its member's container (`sc_kept`; `sc_name_container`, D-3b's container of a name's member, now made from a binding and a name), and a call of a method that returns a member reads that name's container (`sc_kept_returned`); `this` is every class method's receiver (`sc_this_value`; B-3 gave it to a class made in several places only), given at a call (`sc_class_method`); `collected_members` takes the members of `this` a module puts anything into, so a name's member of that name is read as a container from its first reading. A call on anything but a name puts nothing anywhere, so the loader's `this._moduleManager.getRecorder().record(…)` keeps nothing. Tests: `supply.rs` (`a_method_keeps_what_it_is_given_for_its_receiver`: a flush through `this`, a method of its own, a method returning the member, the member, the instance, a module-level call in a function; not another instance's, not a constant; received code kept and run; D-3b's recorder case now reads another instance), `test_supply_chain_signals` (`KeptMemberTests`: 6 cases). Measured, before and after (`$S/d3c/measure.py`): the four tree calls on the 21,786 JavaScript files of the npm packages installed here (`/opt/node2x`, `/opt/node-tools`, `/opt/npm-tools`, the global ones) and on monaco-editor 0.57.0's 1,603: no answer changed; monaco-editor's scan the same (INCOMPLETE here, the budget, as before); the popular set's 1,703 releases moved no verdict and no finding (two the harness timed out on, rerun alone, as before). - **A set's misses described without their contents: `bench.py misses`** (Oct 15; John asked for stories on the benchmark's misses, which no session may open, and chose this command over a one-off script; no rule set change). `scripts/bench.py misses SETS.json NAME` (new): `misses_run` reads the set's last finished run (`RESULTS/history.json`, or `--label`'s), refuses a set marked `aggregate_only`, and scans again in memory each malicious release of the run that is not SUSPICIOUS. `describe_miss` hands the archive's members to `repo.scan_members` through `_observed`, which notes each file's path, size and kind and keeps the bytes of the release's own manifest, and writes one line: the run's verdict and the scan's with its reason, every finding's rule, severity, file, line and message (never its snippet), the files, the unused dependencies, what SC-USE-RISK read, and `_package_facts` (npm: package.json read as the scan reads it, its install hooks' commands, the other scripts' names and each dependency field with its ranges; PyPI: the METADATA's or PKG-INFO's Name, Version and each Requires-Dist), each value cut at `MISS_TEXT` characters; it prints counts only. `test_bench_script.py`'s `MissesTests`: a WARN npm release with a hook and a binary file and a wheel with a `.pth` file described, a SUSPICIOUS and a benign release left out, a marker in every file that never reaches the output, the holdout and the mistakes refused. Owed: John's run of it on run `0b174164`'s benchmark (76 misses: 32 WARN, 44 OK).
- **`datadog.py`: a sample zipped inside itself** (Oct 15; found by `bench.py misses` on run `0b174164`, John: "Yes let's update that script then"; no rule set change). litellm 1.82.8's sample at `0cf258a4` is a zip whose release is nothing but another zip, named as the dataset names its samples; the release rules packed that zip into an sdist, so the benchmark's "litellm 1.82.8" was one embedded archive (WARN on SC-NESTED-ARCHIVE) and no scan saw the release. `release` now reads such a zip as the sample (`zipped_inside`: the release's files are one zip named as a sample is, `SAMPLE_NAME`, or as this sample, encrypted or not; `MAX_WRAPS` = 2 times, then skipped), before the npm and PyPI rules; a zip named for the package stays the release's own archive, and any other lone zip a file. Each record keeps `rules` (`RULES` = 2): a run makes again the releases recorded under older rules (`make_release`), removes a file of the old rules that the new one does not overwrite (`drop_replaced`), and says how many were made again and changed (the benchmark's by id). `test_datadog_script.py`: a sample zipped inside itself once and twice, encrypted or not, beside its package_info or alone, named as a sample or as itself; three times skipped; another lone zip and a PyPI release's own `.zip` sdist unchanged; a run whose wrapped sample comes out as its release; records of the old rules made again, one changed, its old file removed, the others' bytes the same, and nothing read on the next run. Owed: John's run of `datadog.py` (it remakes the 1,264 releases recorded before: in the benchmark litellm 1.82.8 should change, and its three skipped samples are tried again, as every run tries them), then `bench.py all`.

- **SECRET-FP: the secret rules on John's discovery run** (Oct 15, night; John: "I do think you should look at the raw info re secrets to see if we need to hone that code"; rule set 2.83.0). His run's database (465 scans) held 30 S-SECRET/S-TOKEN BLOCKERs and 238 S-ENTROPY MAJORs, none a credential: Hugging Face repositories, feature flags, link and RPC names, media types, placeholders and environment variables' names, AWS's documentation key, unsigned test JWTs, alphabets, Ethereum addresses, base64 of documents. All in the engine, nothing in Python or the page (John: "stop making dual copies that we are going to make go away", and "It is a cross engine copy if rust will be replacing it"). `findings.rs`: `entropy_secretish` passes over `identifier_like` (few changes of shape between letters and digits, at most a run of digits in ten characters, a separator or two capitalised words and then mostly lowercase, vowels as words have them, no `=` and a `+` only before a media type's suffix), `sequence_run` of 8 or more, `evm_address`, `encoded_document` (base64 whose first bytes are printable text starting `<`, `{` or `[`). `config.rs`: `secret_value_in_code` (S-SECRET's value: the config reader's test with blanks allowed, `_SRC_PLACEHOLDER_RE` without the words a weak password holds, `_SRC_QUALIFIED_NAME_RE` with parts of at most 30 characters, so a JWT is not a name, `_SRC_CAMEL_NAME_RE`) and `sample_token` (documentation samples, `EXAMPLE`/`FAKE`/`DUMMY`/`SAMPLE` in the token, a JWT's signature missing, under 16 characters or letters alone), also for config files' S-TOKEN. `scanfile.rs`: `secret_col` (each match's value, and a `PWD` that is the command or a Windows path), `token_col` with `sample_token`. The pack: `SECRET_SKIP_RE` and S-SECRET's skip matched as words, case by case (`(?<![A-Za-z0-9])example(?![a-z])`, `Example`, `EXAMPLE`: `exampleKey` is one, `mockingbird` is not), `canary` and `made-up` added; SECRET_SKIP_RE also passes over a line that names a public key (`publicKey`, `PUBLIC_KEY`, `pubkey`; not `public` alone, a TypeScript field's modifier). Checked: the run's 145 flagged lines scanned again (S-SECRET 0 of 18, S-TOKEN 0 of 12, S-ENTROPY 5 of 115: three unnamed public keys and a generated name); the popular set's 1,703 releases, every source file in dependency mode (`$SP/secfp/measure_pop.py`): S-SECRET 480 to 87, S-TOKEN 59 to 55, S-ENTROPY 8,208 to 582, each removed kind read (the `windows` crates' feature names 6,290, `aws-lc-sys`'s link names, Kubernetes' and Google's type and RPC names, alphabets, MIME types, doc placeholders, test passwords under 8 characters), nothing new; 4,000 random keys of each of five alphabets (`keyloss.py`): of those flagged before, none of the 32-character base62 and 43-character base64 ones lost, 1 of 3,999 base64url and 2 of 2,620 20-character base62 ones; 191 lines of random real-shaped credentials in 20 contexts (`battery.py`): all still reported but for the choices below and a JWT whose signature is under 16 characters; the snapshot corpora's removed S-TOKEN findings each a sample (fuzz and fixture JWTs with a short signature, 429 of 434; four `…example…` tokens; AWS's key). Chosen, and in the CHANGELOG: a dash-joined passphrase reads as a name (as in config files), and S-SECRET's value is at least 8 characters. The npm package's `report.js` `buildResult` and `regrade` and `metrics.js` `computeMetrics` the same (`metricsFiles`, `findingRows`); `worstSevRating`, `maintainabilityRating`, `COVERAGE_RULES`, `DUP_LANGS`, `sortIssues`, `severityGrades` and `rules.js`' `HARDENING_RULES` gone, the first two no longer exported. Measured: `build_result` on this repository's `python/src` (72 files, 602 findings) 0.097 to 0.096 s (best of five; the file metrics are most of it). The dashboard keeps its own `buildResult` until step 5.

- **BR-7, step 4 (b): decoding is the engine's, every codec Python has** (Oct 16; John, Oct 15: "Let's prioritize the single engine story and start on it now. I do want the codec work done"; rule set 2.84.0: files whose cookie names a codec npm could not decode are now decoded). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/decode/` (new): `mod.rs` (the BOM and NUL sniff, a BOM-less UTF-16 guess kept when it reads as text, the PEP 263 cookie on line 1 or 2, the codec as the interpreter resolves the name through `ALIASES`, the encoding findings, decode_member's test that a file reads as text and its MPEG-TS exception; the calls `decode_source` {lang, issues, redact, name, windows}, `encoding_issues` {info, redact} and `decode` {codec}), `utf.rs` (UTF-8's maximal subparts, UTF-16 and -32 with and without a byte order mark, UTF-7 as RFC 2152 and Python's decoder read it), `escape.rs` (unicode_escape and raw_unicode_escape, strict, `\N{name}` not decoded), `cjk.rs` (the 16 stateless CJK codecs as trees of tables, read byte by byte from Python's incremental decoder; gb18030's four-byte form by its linear index; EUC-KR's KS X 1001:1998 make-up sequences) and `iso2022.rs` (the seven ISO-2022 codecs and HZ: G0 to G2, SO and SI, single shift 2, the escape sequences' scan, "ESC throughout", the JIS X 0208-1990 announcer and any six-byte escape that ends in ESC $ B), written from the RFCs and from what Python's decoders do on probes; where Python's decoder raises (iso2022_jp_2's single shift 2 with JIS X 0201 Roman in G2: RuntimeError) the file is read as UTF-8, as core read it. `scripts/make_codec_tables.py` writes `generated/codecs.rs` from Python's decoders (the aliases; the single-byte tables; the CJK trees, 162,000 entries run-length coded; the ISO-2022 codecs' designations, one-byte and single-shift sets, and two-byte sets as another codec's characters with the few that differ; byte for byte the same on Python 3.10, 3.11, 3.12, 3.13 and 3.14) and still the dashboard's copy of the single-byte tables; `CODEC_ALIAS_EXTRAS`, `HOST_CODECS` and `CHARMAP_FIXES` are the generator's. The packages: core's `decode_source`, `encoding_issues` and `decode_member` ask the engine (decode_member in one call); `detect_encoding`, `_python_cookie`, `_normal_codec`, `charmap_table`, `_decode_escapes`, `_text_is_plausible`, `_undecodable_share` and their tables gone. The npm package's `lib/codecs.js` deleted and `lib/encoding.js` (342 lines) down to file names and `decodeSource` over the engine; `fs.js`' `decodeMember` one call, `encodingIssues`, `undecodableShare` and `percent` gone. Notices: `rust/NOTICE` lists `generated/codecs.rs` as Unicode data (the vendors' tables named), `js/NOTICE` no longer lists `codecs.js`. Tests: `test_decode_engine.py` (new: core's code as it was kept as the reference, decoding every codec with Python's codecs, held to the engine on every codec under its spellings, 1,500 generated files read as Python, JavaScript and unknown, their encoding findings redacted and not, 3,600 members read by `decode_member`, each codec's every byte and random bytes, where Python raises; the WebAssembly build's answers the library's), `test_decode_engine_codecs.py` (new: every two bytes of the 16 CJK, 8 stateful and the UTF-16 and UTF-7 codecs, gb18030's four-byte and EUC-KR's eight-byte forms, the ISO-2022 escapes with each byte after them, six-byte escapes that end in ESC $ B); `decode/mod.rs`' own (the sniff's table, the plausibility test, Python's percentages, the info read back); `test_review_codecs` (the generator current; the dashboard on the codecs it decodes; a CJK cookie decoded, idna not), `test_review_escape_codecs` (the engine against Python's codecs case by case), `test_review_encoding`, `test_detection_gaps`, `test_notices`, `test_rust_notices`; the npm tests of the removed functions gone, their scans kept (`review-codecs`, `review-escape-codecs`, `review-nul-sniff`). Before the commit, a harness over the `decode` call: every codec's every one and two bytes, every three bytes after a held two (a sample of 1,024 held prefixes where there are more), gb18030's 1.6 million four-byte sequences, EUC-KR's make-up sequences, every ISO-2022 escape of up to four bytes with each control and byte after it and every one of up to six bytes over the 12 bytes its scan tells apart, 20,000 random strings for every codec and 300,000 for each stateful one: no difference from Python. The first ISO-2022 and UTF-7 decoders written for this step were ports of CPython's C code, which P-16 does not allow (the engine is Lazaret's own and no package carries CPython's license): they were written again from the RFCs and the probes before the commit, and so were gb18030's four-byte and EUC-KR's make-up forms; the harness held the new ones to Python (and found the JIS X 0208-1990 announcer, ESC & @, which the first new decoder had missed, and, in the random strings, that Python takes any six-byte escape sequence that ends in ESC $ B as the announced JIS X 0208). Measured: `decode_member` over this repository's 329 source files (13.1 MB) 0.32 to 0.40 s. The engine grows by 0.5 MiB (the WebAssembly module 6.30 to 6.80 MB, 2.16 to 2.36 MB gzipped; the native library 11.1 to 11.7 MB). The dashboard keeps its decoding until step 5.

- **BR-7, step 5: the dashboard runs the engine** (Oct 16; John, Oct 15: "Prioritize all of the single engine updates -- stop making dual copies that we are going to make go away", and Oct 16, on where the page's engine lives: "Committed in the page"; no rule set change). `js/dashboard/` (new): `page.html` (the markup and styles, the engine's data block before the script, the notices), `page.js` (the uploads, read as far as the collection looks into them: a file over 2,000,001 bytes only its first 2,048; the paste as `pasted-code.<py|js|sql>`, its UTF-8 bytes; `scanEntries`: each through `collectEntry`, then `collectionIssues`, `gradeResult`, `redactResult`; the UI on the CLI's result: `conditions`, a card for the cross-file flows; the export `lazaret-dashboard-2`) and `wasmload.js` (the engine from the data block: base64, `DecompressionStream`, `WebAssembly.compile`, and two instances made ahead, since a browser instantiates a module this size only asynchronously and `native.js` asks for one synchronously, after a trap). `scripts/make_dashboard.py` (new): the page's modules, each in a function of its own in import order (named and namespace imports, `export` declarations, lists and re-exports; a `node:` or package import, a default import, `import.meta`, `import()`, `export let` and a cycle refused), `src/lib/wasmload.js` replaced by the page's, the CSP (`script-src` the script's hash and `'wasm-unsafe-eval'`), the engine (`--engine`: gzip, base64 in lines of 100, `data-sha256`, `data-bytes` and `data-sources`, the SHA-256 of the engine's sources: `rust/Cargo.toml`, `Cargo.lock`, `lazaret-ffi` and its path dependencies, `js/scripts/build-wasm.js`), kept while those sources are unchanged; `--check`. The npm package: `lib/collect.js` (new; out of `fs.js`: `EXTS`, `DEP_LANGS`, `VENDOR_CODE`, `neverBuilt`, `GYP_EXTS`, `DEP_MARKERS`, `MAX_FILE_BYTES`, `configSkippedIssue`, `scriptSourceLang`, and `collectFile` as `collectEntry(entry, col)`, an entry reading its bytes through `read` and `head`), `scancollection.js` (new; out of `project.js`: `collectionIssues`, the CLI's hook and --deps checks given as `checks`), `result.js` (new; out of `report.js`: `gradeResult`, `regrade`, `localIso`), `lib/wasmload.js` (new; out of `native.js`), `issue.js` (`truncatedIssue`, `scanErrorIssue`), `native.js` (`bytesText`; the path separator without `process` in a browser); `fs.js`' second copies of `normalizeNewlines` and `decodeMember` gone. no rule set change). `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/pyflow/mod.rs` (`Reads`, `Changed`, `Project::epoch`, `note_*`, `mark_*`, `unchanged`: what a reading of the fixpoint reads of the state readings change (summaries, classes' attributes, modules' globals, the variables a function gives the functions nested in it and those they give back, the environment a script stores) and when each part last changed; `first_begin`, `first_end`: the work of making what later readings find made: a name resolved, a call's targets, a class's bases, a name classified), `pyflow/driver.rs` (`read` records each reading of the fixpoint; the fixpoint counts the last steps of a function none of whose reads changed instead of reading it), `pyflow/eval.rs` and `pyflow/supply.rs` (the reads noted, the changes marked; `sc_names` once per function and call node with the work it charges, `names_memo`; a function's own nodes walked once, `sc_own_nodes`; the facts' thread given the call's tokens and gate), `pyparse/lexer.rs` (`shared`, `shared_with_tstrings`, `t_prefix_in`: the parser's tokens are the lexers' where the two readings are the same; `ident_char` by a table, `keyword` by length and first letter), `pyparse/mod.rs` (`parse_shared`), `lex/py.rs`, `signs.rs` (`PyLoad::read` on the shared tokens; the decoded view's copy of a text gets a gate of its own, and its searches go to the text's when the two are the same; `literal_spans` once a call, for both threads), `flow.rs` (`dd_assigns_by_hand`: `_DD_ASSIGN_RE`'s matches read by hand, from one place an assignment can start to the next), `textgate.rs` (`share`, `adopt`, `memo_shared`: a gate shared with another thread, for a copy of its text; the pairs, the triples and each pair's count in one pass, the places in one more), `pystr.rs` (`Needles`: an Aho-Corasick automaton for a list of ASCII strings, used on a line and on a long text whose pairs list more than one place in eight), `linre/literal.rs` (`LitSet::pair_places`), `jsflow/supply.rs` (the JavaScript facts' thread shares the gate too). In instructions per call on a warm engine (p1bench: one file, a call on a variant of it after another; `next` before P-1, `9b421a37`, measured the same way): `proxy_server.py` 1,546 M before P-1, 1,024 M before this part, 764 M now (2.02×); `coreBundle.js` 5,815 M, 3,457 M, 2,860 M (2.03×); `utilsBundle.js` 8,580 M, 4,680 M, 4,124 M (2.08×). (The earlier parts' table measured with another harness, 1,660 M, 6,494 M and 9,421 M before P-1, reads 7 to 10% higher.) Held to the engine before it on 6,790 files (litellm 1.103.2's sdist, botocore, requests, next, eslint, express, TypeScript; seven of the engine's calls on each): the same answers, and with the model's readings skipped the steps the Python pass counted, its fixpoint's and its reporting's, the same on every file. - **SH-COMMENT-FP: a shell script's comments are not its commands** (Oct 16; backlog SH-COMMENT-FP, P1; John: "Go ahead and start those"; rule set 2.88.0). The install-script test read a shell script whole and the shell reader (`_sh_parse`) had no notion of a comment, so agent-sanitizer's `session-setup.sh`, whose comment quotes the `curl https://webi.sh/... | sh` it replaced, made five of its hooks SC-AUTORUN CRITICAL. `rust/crates/lazaret-engine/src/shell.rs`: `sh_comments_blanked` (a `#` that starts a word, outside quotes, `$'...'`, a here-document's body and arithmetic, to the end of its line, each character a space: offsets and lines kept; the `#!` line kept; None when nothing is blanked or the text does not read to its end), `sh_reads_own_file` (`$0`, `${0...}`, `$BASH_SOURCE`, `$ZSH_ARGZERO` or a variable assigned one of them or what `realpath`, `readlink` or `basename` make of it, given to a program not in `_SH_SELF_PATH_TOOLS` or read with `<`, in the command or a substitution of it), `script_without_comments` (a `#!` line naming a shell, or no language and a text that does not read as code: `shell_text`). `signs.rs`: `install_script_risk_with` reads a shell script (`shell`, not a command) without its comments. The pack: `_SH_SELF_PATH_TOOLS`, `_SH_SELF_RESOLVERS`, `_SH_SELF_MAX_DEPTH`. Measured: the popular repositories' agent files, the engine before and after: agent-sanitizer's five CRITICAL are INFO and its gate passes, nothing else changed (SC-AUTORUN 10 CRITICAL and 150 INFO before, 5 and 155 after); the popular set's 1,703 releases, run before and after in the sandbox: no verdict, strong, weak or use-time finding changed. Tests: `shell.rs` (`comment_tests`: what is blanked, what is not, a text read whole, a script that reads its own file), `signs.rs` (`comment_tests`), `test_shell_comments.py` (10) and `js/test/shell-comments.test.js` (the npm package's twin); `test_snapshot_hooks` recorded again after review (74 of 44,893 outputs, each an install-script answer that lost a reason a comment gave: a generated text whose `#` starts a word; none gained one). Docs: DESIGN, README, CHANGELOG, RUST_ENGINE.
- **GYP-SLOW: unclosed command expansions read in linear time and memory** (Oct 16; backlog GYP-SLOW, P2; John: "Go ahead and start those"; no rule set change). `review-gyp.test.js`'s 20,000 unclosed expansions in one string took 7 to 8 s on a quiet two-core machine at every commit measured from BR-7 step 4 (f) to RA-2. Profiled: `manifest/gyp.rs`'s `expansion_commands` copied each expansion's command, and an unclosed one's runs to the end of the string, so 20,000 of them were some 600 million characters (2.4 GB as code points) before `commands` read them up to `_GYP_MAX_COMMAND_CHARS` (2,000,000: 34 of them, then SC-TRUNCATED). It now returns where each command is, (start, end, pymod), and `commands` copies only what it reads. The same findings (the scan's JSON the same, by hash); natively 1.39 s and 2.3 GB at the peak before, 0.24 s and 52 MB after; in WebAssembly 14.1 s before and 0.9 s after, on a machine running the Python suite beside it. Tests: `gyp.rs` (`an_unclosed_expansions_command_runs_to_the_end_of_its_string`: the places, and the characters copied bounded), `review-gyp.test.js` and `test_review_gyp.py` as they were (the comment updated).
- **PY-DECODE-FIRST: a string the parser cannot decode is the error where Python's is** (Oct 16; found while GH-SCAN-ALL's suite ran: its new Python files changed `test_pyparse_native_b`'s seeded mutations of the repository's own sources, and one, `"\U0001F60\", "\U0001D41A"` in a list opened four lines before, had Python say "'[' was never closed" at line 16 and the engine "unexpected character after line continuation character" at line 20; no rule set change). Python decodes a string's escapes in its parser's action for the STRING token, before its tokenizer reads the next token; its error then stands, except that Python tokenizes the rest of the text and a tokenizer error with a bracket open from an earlier line makes that bracket's error (`_PyPegen_tokenize_full_source_to_check_for_errors`). The engine's `strings` took the token first, so the read had reached the backslash's token, whose error won. `pyparse/expr.rs`: the string decoded before `advance`; `parser.rs`: `decode_at`, the token where the read stopped for `error`. Tests: `pyparse/tests.rs` (`which_error_python_reports`: two brackets never closed, a string's own error on its line), `test_pyparse_native_b` on the tree with GH-SCAN-ALL's files.
- **GH-SCAN-ALL: every new GitHub repository, and GH Archive's events** (Oct 16; John: "Let's do build 1 and two and ensure our help for scan shows examples", after his first `discover --ecosystem github` runs kept nothing of 633). `registry/ghevents.py` (new): GH Archive's hourly files (`data.gharchive.org/YYYY-MM-DD-H.json.gz`, the host alone, no credential; `fetch`: None for a 404, an hour not published yet; `read`: gzip or the lines themselves, within `ARCHIVE_BYTES`, `TEXT_BYTES` and `LINE_BYTES`, `PushEvent` and a branch or tag's `CreateEvent` as code, a repository's `CreateEvent` with its description, `PublicEvent`; `keep` says whose pushes are kept). `ghdiscover.py`: `run(archive=, scan_all=, max_scans=, archive_http=)`; `_checks`, `_archive` (the cursor `gharchive`, the first run the last complete hour, `ARCHIVE_HOURS` a run, an hour still unpublished `ARCHIVE_GIVE_UP` hours after it ended passed over as a gap, an unreadable hour stops the archive's part alone), `_apply` (a push makes a waiting candidate ready; a repository made public through the filters, or queued with scan-all; scan-all's pushes to repositories newer than the cursor `github-scan-all` queued, or `pending` above the listing's cursor), `_judge` (campaign markers and excludes on GH Archive's name and description), `_scan_all_listed` (the listing queues, reports or excludes a pending repository, and keeps your excludes as `excluded`), `_scans` (`--max-scans`: ready candidates oldest first, then the queue newest first; a queued repository's failed scan drops it), the queue kept at `QUEUE_MAX` and pending ones `PENDING_HOURS`; `Filters.judged`, `excluded`. The store (repo.py): `add_github_candidates` (one transaction), `github_candidate_states`, `prune_github_candidates`, `github_candidates(newest_first=, limit=)`. The CLI: `--github-archive`, `--github-scan-all`, `--max-scans` (refused without `discover --ecosystem github`), the run's GH Archive and scan-all lines and "kept 0", `list` summing scan-all's rows; examples in `lazaret-registry --help` (`HELP_EXAMPLES`), `lazaret --help` (`core.SCAN_EXAMPLES`) and the npm package's `--help`. `.gitignore`: `lazaret-registry.db`. Not measured live: the sandbox's proxy refuses data.gharchive.org (the hour's format is GH Archive's documented one, the Events API's events). Tests: `test_ghdiscover_archive.py` (22: reading an hour, the fetch, the archive's cursor and its gaps, readiness, made public, scan-all's queue, pending, excluded, campaigns never downloaded, the scans' cap and order, the queue's bound, the pending repositories' expiry, the CLI, the help, Postgres's statements). Docs: DESIGN §5l, README, CHANGELOG.
- **GH-SCAN-ALL, after John's first run** (Oct 17, 00:03; John: "Do what makes sense"). His run of Oct 16, 23:57: 20,100 repositories listed (203 pages, caught up), kept 0; GH Archive's hour 2026-10-17-3 read (the format as documented, 6 repositories made public) but only 4,859 events, where GitHub's public timeline has some 150,000 an hour: GH Archive has missed most events since 2025 (gharchive.org's issues #320 and #323, the GitHub community discussion #178788), and OpenDigger mirrors the same files since September 2026; 5 of the 6 made public scanned OK and one, gone before its scan (`@HEAD: not found`), made the run exit 1; each OK scan printed its quality findings. `ghevents.py`: `source` (an https host and a folder: the default port, no credential, query or fragment), `DEFAULT_SOURCE`, `MIRROR`, `THIN_HOUR`; `fetch` and `url` take the source. `ghdiscover.py`: `run(events_url=, progress=)`, the archive's `source`, `default` and `thin`; a scan answered 404 or 409 (`GONE`) drops its candidate and counts `gone`, no error; progress before each hour, every `LISTING_PROGRESS_EVERY` pages and before each scan. repo.py: `--github-events-url` (env `LAZARET_GITHUB_EVENTS_URL`, refused without `discover --ecosystem github`), `_github_progress` (stderr), the thin hour's line and the mirror's hint, the gone count, OK scans one line each (the rest in full), the help's example. GH Archive stays the default (John's call, delegated: the mirror is newer and a third party's; the run says when an hour is thin). Tests: `test_ghdiscover_archive.py` (28: the source, the mirror read, the thin hour, progress, gone and empty repositories, the CLI's OK lines and a scan in full, the option's check).
- The scheduled long runs are `perf.yml` (P-10) and `fuzz.yml` (X-1), in files of their own, not `ci.yml` (that is the 0.1.8 agent's until the tag); both are written, checked with `zizmor` and in `.github/workflows/` on the branch. A schedule runs only from the default branch, so the first real runs are after the merge. The Rust lexer fuzz's short gate already runs in `cargo test`; `check_rust_deps.py` refuses dev-dependencies, so no `cargo-fuzz`.
- The rules that read the spans of the hooks (`rsparse::hooks` and `goparse::hooks`: what a `#[ctor]`, a `build.rs` `main`, an `init()` or a cgo preamble does), in new files, registered after the tag. Both parsers and the hooks are done.
- V-1's second stage (`lazaret scan --verify-secrets`): now, before the tag (John, Oct 7: decisions 4 and 7; plan `specs/lazaret-v1-stage2-plan-2026-10-07.md`). Steps 1 to 4 are done (above): the table and the logic in the engine, Python's call over lazaret-net, `lazaret scan --verify-secrets`, the npm runner; step 5, the docs, came with each step. Owed before the release: `scripts/verifylive` with real or test keys (an Anthropic `sk-ant-api03-` key and an OpenAI key among them: the 401 bodies the table now reads are from the providers' documentation).

## Findings channel

Problems the fuzzers or the new code find in the 0.1.8 agent's files are written to `docs/0.1.9-findings.md` as reproducible notes (seed, iteration, smallest input). Each entry is mirrored into a Lazaret.dev project doc, because the 0.1.8 agent sees this worktree only through read-only git. Entries move to the CHANGELOG at release.

## Backlog (profile of Oct 3)

Full profile, tables and the prioritized backlog: the Lazaret.dev project, `audits/lazaret-profile-and-backlog-2026-10-03.md`. In short, on the 2-vCPU sandbox:

- The engine is 37 to 89% of a registry scan; archive reading 2 to 12% (botocore 41%); other Python 6 to 19% on the big packages. Porting `repo.py` or `guard.py` to Rust does not pay now.
- `import_time_risk` runs at 3 to 5 MB/s (3 to 6 times slower than `scan_file`) and is 54 to 80% of engine time. The review of the profile (`audits/lazaret-profile-review-2026-10-03.md`) found why: its data-flow analysis (60% of a Python file's call), not re-reading files. SC-USE-RISK reads files for at most 3 s per artifact, so on next it reads 28% of its input; findings on large bundles depend on machine speed.
- litellm ships 8 release files (1.102.0, 1.103.0 and 1.103.2 alike); 25% of their bytes are unique, so dedupe by content hash within a run cuts that scan from 76 s to about 26 s (estimate). Adjacent versions repeat 51 to 92% of bytes (the persistent cache, which must not be trusted across refs).
- `guard pip` runs pip twice: 19.95 s against 6.23 s plain, scanning 1.9 s of it. `guard npm`, cold: 44.3 s against 19.8 s plain; with the verdict cache 20.5 s.

| Pri | Item |
|---|---|
| P1 | P-14 SC-USE-RISK bounded by work, not wall time (first after the tag: it takes the raised box out of later gates); P-1 speed up `import_time_risk` (levers in the review; after the tag); P-13 carry batch texts outside the JSON (about 7% of a scan) |
| P1 | P-2a dedupe within a run by content hash |
| P1 | P-3 `guard pip` installs from the checked plan |
| P1 | P-4 `--timings` in `lazaret-registry` and `guard` |

| P1 | S-1 CLI `scan github:` / `gitlab:`; S-2 Actions at pinned SHAs plus the offline GitHub workflow checks (both twins); S-3 GitLab CI checks (with an npm twin); S-4 merge and wire-up (first after the tag) |
| P1 | G-1 Go secrets, `init()` detectors, proxy auditor, `go.sum` SCA; R-1 Rust `build.rs` detectors, crates.io auditor, `Cargo.lock` SCA |
| P2 | P-2b persistent cache; P-6 one engine call per file (the double parse stays in P-1); P-7 guard pool memory and guard isolation (done on the 0.1.9 branch: no wall gain, the guard's memory falls from 2.15 GB to 0.22 GB at `--jobs 1`); P-8 the serial parts of an engine scan; P-9 parallel downloads; P-15 guard connection reuse; P-16 Rust-first phase 4 (matcher, Unicode, retire `pyre` and the shlex port); P-10 CI perf gate; G-2/R-2 parsers; G-3/R-3 guards; S-5; X-1 fuzzers; X-2 `repo.py` ecosystem interface (right after P-2a: the Go and crates.io auditors are in 0.1.9, John Oct 3; this branch writes the interface doc first, in wave 2); X-3 VS Code and Open VSX |

Until the 0.1.8 tag this branch edits only the allow-list above; `repo.py`, `engine.py`, `_native.py`, `api.rs` and the rest are the 0.1.8 agent's, in the order the work split gives (P-14 first, bounded by work, then the P-4 call sites, P-2a, X-2, P-13, P-1, P-6 with P-8, P-16, P-2b, P-9). No P-1 lever before the tag. Done on this branch (see above): P-3, P-4's helper, P-7 (with guard isolation), P-5 (the indexed CVE bundle), P-10, S-1, S-2 (both halves, both twins), S-3, X-1 (34 targets), P-15, the P-2 and X-2 design docs, the P-2a memo (`registry/contentcache.py`, not called yet), the registry interface (`base.py`, the conformance mixin), the crates.io and Go modules, the Go oracle, the Go and Rust SCA readers (`go.mod`, `go.sum`, `vendor/modules.txt`, `Cargo.lock`, `Cargo.toml`, with `scanner/gomod.py`) , the Go parser (`goparse/`), the Go and Cargo guards (G-3 and R-3), V-1's first stage (the verifier, the provider table, the transport and AWS signing; not called yet), the Rust item reader (`rsparse/`) and the Go and Rust hooks. Can start now, in new files: the signature and tile check for the Go checksum database and the fuzzers for `pmsettings`' file readers; V-1's second stage waits for the tag and John's decision 6 (the open decisions 6 to 10 are in the work split). The Rust-first plan's phase 5 is replaced by the P-items (P-2a/b, P-6, P-8, P-7, P-11, P-13); phase 4 (linear-time matcher, current Unicode, retiring `pyre` and the shlex port) is the new P-16, the 0.1.8 agent's after the `api.rs` queue (John, Oct 3). The split between the two lanes is in `specs/lazaret-work-split-2026-10-03.md`.
